## iter-121 — the work that is needed under EITHER scope outcome (2026-08-07)

**Type:** tik · `iter_shape: instrument + filing` · **No reading was taken and none is implied. `P` is
UNMEASURED, exactly as after pass 26.** No successor strategy is authored; clause 5 is not re-cut,
reinterpreted, narrowed or argued. **No fourth harden pass was requested** — see §3 below.

### 1. Two PLATFORM findings filed — they are not documentation defects, and the corpus repair did not address them

iter-120 repaired the corpus's flattering security claims. Two of the underlying facts are **platform**
concerns in their own right. Both are now in
[`platform-defect-register.md`](../../platform-defect-register.md) with `file:line` and their measurement,
which takes that file from 5 M257x-era entries to 7 — and it is worth naming that the register **had zero
M257x entries until iter-102**, having been created by M256's deferral audit for exactly this class.

- **`PLATFORM-M257x-graphql-authz-middleware-FAILS-OPEN-and-REST-has-no-blanket-gate`** — the six paths
  that reach the resolver before the single Sentinel call, the one hardcoded target variable, the REST
  surface, and the platform's own post-mortem comment. Re-derived line by line at `app` `ad9f3c49`.
  Carries, **without editorialising**, the impersonation mutation's gate:
  `ActionObjectTaxonomy` / `UserActionWrite` (`resolver_admin_audit.go:20-24`) — a taxonomy-write
  permission rather than a dedicated one; the sibling `adminAuditLogs` query uses the identical pair.
- **`PLATFORM-M257x-dev-login-routes-mint-a-full-session-for-any-email-behind-one-NODE_ENV-boolean`** —
  and **one premise of the routing was re-derived and is FALSE, which is recorded first in the entry
  rather than quietly dropped.**

  > The item came in as *"`ant-academy/…/login-as/route.js:78` has **no `NODE_ENV` gate**."* **It does** —
  > `route.js:34` refuses on `!DEV_LOGIN_ENABLED`, and `code/src/lib/devLogin.js:29` is
  > `NODE_ENV !== 'production'`. The **ungated** site is `next-web-app/e2e/auth.setup.ts:72`, and the
  > *"skips both factors"* comment is that file's (`:57-62`), not ant-academy's. **Two of the five minting
  > sites had been conflated** — which is itself an instance of the class this milestone measures, arriving
  > in the routing rather than in the corpus.

  What was filed instead is what is true, and it is still worth a platform engineer's attention: three of
  the five sites are made **public** (unauthenticated) in middleware by the same boolean that mounts them
  (`ant-academy/code/proxy.js:178`, `next-web-app/apps/web/src/proxy.ts:56`); the email is taken from the
  query string with **no allowlist and no shared secret**; and the whole control is one comparison against
  a **build-mode** variable, not a deployment gate.

### 2. A TENTH quantifier defect — in iter-120's own repair, pointing the other way

iter-120 replaced *"Sentinel validates **every** API request"* with *"the REST surface has **no authz
middleware at all**"*. **Also false.** `cbGate := courseBuilderAccessGate(authorizationManager)`
(`backend.go:227`, defined `internal/web/backend/gate.go:27-49`) IS a Sentinel-backed **group** middleware
(`OrgCheckFeaturePermission(OrgFeatureMembersEdit, orgID)`), applied to `/coursebuilder` (`:229-232`) and
`/credits` (`:273-276`). The repair's own citations — `:230-231` and `:274-275` — **each stop one line
short of it.**

Repaired at all three sites that carried it (`security_compliance.md`, `architecture_overview.md`,
`backend.md`), with the full six-group table replacing the assertion. **Two things this is evidence for,
and they point in opposite directions:** the repair *class* is right — the conclusion (no blanket gate)
survives — and **a repair of an absolute quantifier introduced a new absolute quantifier in one pass.**
Also: a citation that stops one line short of its own subject is precisely the wrong-construct class
`anchor_construct_guard` does not detect, found here by reading rather than by any fence.

### 3. Three instruments closed or floored — see the rext commit for the full derivations

| item | outcome |
|---|---|
| `FIX-M257x-iter108-stackcore-suite-hangs` | **closed as stated** — the ambiguity, not the runtime, was the defect. `progress_beacon.py` + 7 wired sites + an AST-derived wiring fence. §4 below records the standing invocation |
| `FIX-M257x-iter120-anchor-guard-detects-blank-not-wrong` | **DECLINED with a measurement, floor DISCLOSED in the instrument.** Only **28 of 511** backticked citations (5.5 %) supply their own expected content, and binding that content to the right anchor needs the sentence's claim — 1 of 20 measurably binds to a LATER anchor. Fourth decline by this fence family, first with numbers. `KNOWN_WEAKNESS` now prints on every run and rides in `--json` |
| `audit-deferrals` §8 read one field | **closed** — `blocking_state_guard.py` derives over all three blocking-capable fields. It found **8** blocking gradings across 109 graded iters where the audit named **3**; `deferrals-audit.md` §12 enumerates all eight with dispositions, and §8's banner is superseded with **Q5** written out |

**Standing rules added** (they outlive the milestone): `platform-alignment.md` §5 **rules 51, 52, 53**.

### 3b. The whole-suite claim, and it is now defensible — with the invocation it rests on

```
cd .agentspace/rosetta-extensions/stack-core
STACKCORE_PROGRESS_LOG=/tmp/m257x-beacon2.log \
  /usr/bin/python3 -m pytest tests/ -q --tb=line -p no:cacheprovider --no-header --durations=5
      ->  1 failed · 1125 passed  in  1032.57 s (17:12)   [rext 1bb64c3, corpus mid-iter-121]
```

`/usr/bin/python3` is **3.9.6** and is the only interpreter on this host with pytest. The single failure is
`test_claim_twin_guard_iter48_answer_key::test_02` — the standing, documented RED, **and it is now
genuinely re-attested by a full run** rather than carried as *"unchanged, not re-verified"* for a fourth
consecutive pass. **239 beacon lines** were emitted; the formerly-silent stretch is now eight timestamped
nested-run pairs.

**The expected wall time is ~1030 s and it reconciles with iter-111's 1090.88 s to within 6 %** — which is
the check rule 51 asks for, and it did not pass on the first attempt. **The FIRST run today returned
632.20 s with 4 failures, and the 460 s gap IS the defect** (§3c): a battery that dies on its baseline
never runs its mutants, so `test_01_every_mutant_matches_its_DECLARED_verdict` cost **45 s** while broken
and **405.69 s** once fixed. *A fast suite is not evidence of a healthy one* — iter-111's own words,
re-measured. **That first run was also tree-edited mid-run and is disclosed as confounded**, exactly as
iter-111 disclosed its run A; the 1032.57 s reading above is the clean one.

### 3c. The first whole-suite run found a RED that four iters of scoped runs could not

**Not introduced by this iter, and that was PROVEN rather than argued:** re-run in a detached worktree at
`b9bb2b6` (iter-121's parent) → identical 3 failures, identical named test. Worktree removed and pruned;
rext stayed on `main`.

`corpus_citation_guard.py` (FENCE-M257x-iter117) joined the participating baseline in
`repair_postcondition_baseline.json` and was **never added** to the mechanical-fences battery's
`_COPY_FILES`. `discover_fences()` globs `*_guard.py` on disk, so the staged tree found **4** participating
fences while its own staged baseline named **5** →
`test_21_the_shipped_baseline_records_EVERY_participating_fence` RED → **ANTI-THEATRE #1 reports a RED
BASELINE**, i.e. *"the fence is broken"* for *"you forgot a file."* **This is iter-111's defect recurring
four iters later on a different file** (§8 rule 7's recurrence corollary: the class, not the site).

Fixed, and the second half is the one that matters: `test_000_the_copy_list_stages_every_fence_the_baseline_names`
is the **ABSENCE direction** iter-111 booked as `FIX-M257x-iter111-staged-battery-dependency-is-underived`
and deferred as *"an import graph, not a grep."* **For this battery it is not an import graph** — the
staged tree carries its own baseline, the baseline names the fences, and discovery is a glob. Battery:
3 failed / 1 passed / 1 skipped → **6 passed in 433.27 s**.

**This is the argument for the beacon, made by the tree rather than by me.** The defect was live for four
iters and three harden passes, every one of which ran scoped suites and each of which recorded *"no
whole-suite total is quoted anywhere in this entry."* The first whole-suite run after the silence was made
readable found it in one pass.

### 3d. Guard family after the corpus edits

**21 members · 17 GREEN · 0 RED · 4 not-run** (`anchor_offset_guard`, `repair_leak_guard`,
`repair_reach_guard`, `value_change_guard` — the commit- and input-scoped members, no `--range`/`--ledger`
supplied). **Not a whole-family green, and the runner's own summary line says so.** The family grew 20 → 21
with `blocking_state_guard`. Invocation: `guard_family.py --repo-root <rosetta> --platform
<rosetta>/stack-demo/platform`.

### 4. The harden cap fired at 3-in-a-row (22, 25, 26) — recorded as a standing finding, NOT a fourth pass

Pass 26 gave the sound reason not to repeat in the same mode: *only mutating the named mechanism surfaces
this class, and running the suite never will.* That is now **rule 53**, together with the pass's
load-bearing method finding — **three of its own mutations silently failed to apply and each read as "the
controls survive"** — which is why every mutation added this iter asserts `count == 1` before its result
is interpreted, and why the beacon mutant removes **both** calls (removing one leaves the site still
beaconing, i.e. a mutant that does not isolate the property).

## iter-122 — the CLAIM CENSUS: the instrument the user funded (2026-08-07)

**Type:** tik · `iter_shape: instrument + census + adjudication` · **No reading was taken and none is
implied. `P` is UNMEASURED.** Clause 5 is not re-cut, reinterpreted, narrowed or argued, and **the gate
did not move — it stands at 4 of 5.**

**The user ruled.** Given the honest choice — close at 4 of 5 with clause 5 documented as
measured-and-unmet, or fund a materially different instrument — they chose the instrument. iter-122
built it, and **sealed its design and its six falsifications before a line of it existed**
(`1262ca7`), exactly as `TOK-07` and `TOK-08` were sealed. The full result is
[`iter-122/adjudication.md`](iter-122/adjudication.md); the sealed design is
[`iter-122/pre-registration.md`](iter-122/pre-registration.md).

### 1. The headline is a FAILED BAND, and it failed on the low side

`F5` sealed the tier-1 `DOES-NOT-SUPPORT` rate at **≥ 4 % and ≤ 25 %**, built from iter-119's
*"measured error rate on the judged set is ≥ 13.3 %"*. Measured over **427 decidable** adjudications:
**3 = 0.70 %**, and **1 = 0.23 %** once two verdicts are traced to the census's own stale substrate (§4).

**A hunted sample over-states the population rate by roughly nineteen-fold.** A reading stops on the
sentences that look wrong, so the rate among sentences a hunter stopped on was never the rate in the
population. **Every error rate this milestone published from a graded read is a rate over a hunted
sample** — that is a fact about the milestone's own prior measurements, and it was obtainable only by
censusing what a reading sampled.

The other two bands held: tier-2 count **1,151** (band 200–2,500); tier-1 pairs **2,603** (band
900–3,000).

### 2. TIER 1 — 525 of 525 adjudicated, exhaustive over the class it names

**2,603** (claiming unit × citation) pairs over the 40 files, across **932 of 2,485** claiming units.
**695** carry a line pin; **525** materialized; **all 525 were adjudicated** by 12 blind adjudicators,
**525 rows for 525 items, 0 seats lost**, verdicts committed verbatim before aggregation.

Clean tally (41 quarantined, §4): **`SUPPORTS` 407 · `PARTIAL` 17 · `DOES-NOT-SUPPORT` 3 ·
`UNRESOLVABLE` 57.**

**The dominant non-`SUPPORTS` class is not error — it is UNCHECKABILITY.** 57 of 484 (11.8 %) are pinned
to commits this tree is not at, with the construct alive at a different line (`app/main.go`'s handler
registrations pinned `@ b948604f` are ~110 lines adrift at `ad9f3c49`). **A re-pin backlog, not a
retraction backlog — and nineteen times the larger finding.**

**One genuine wrong-construct citation, found and repaired:** `academy-backend.md:62` cited
`app/main.go:471-472` for a construction at `:524-525` — **53 lines short of its own subject**, on a
clone level with `origin/main`, so no substrate excuse. `app`'s own `CLAUDE.md` names `:524`/`:525`.

**`F2` FIRES for tier 1 as a whole and is reported with arithmetic rather than a flattering number:**
525 of 2,603 = **20.2 %**; extrapolating ≈ **60 adjudicator runs / ~10.4 M subagent tokens**, and the
1,908 unadjudicated pairs are *harder* (826 bare shas, 525 unpinned files, 507 doc-links). **The honest
form is "100 % of a named 525-pair class", never "20 % of the corpus checked."**

### 3. TIER 2 — the half no fence in this family had ever looked at

Denominator named per `F6`: **3,292 assertion candidates** in the 40 files.

| | count | share |
|---|---|---|
| CITED (a citation in its block) | 2,117 | 64.3 % |
| UNCITED but **HEDGED** | **24** | **0.7 %** |
| UNCITED and **UNHEDGED** → the defect | **1,151** | **35.0 %** |

**1,175 factual assertions carry no citation; 1,151 of them — 98.0 % — carry no hedge either.** The
iter-093 principle is honoured in **24 places out of 1,175 opportunities**. They live in **975 blocks
across 39 of the 40 files**, enumerated line by line in
[`iter-122/raw/tier2-unevidenced-assertions.tsv`](iter-122/raw/tier2-unevidenced-assertions.tsv).

**UNEVIDENCED, never FALSE**, and **a FLOOR** — block scope exonerates a whole paragraph on one citation.
Both disclosures print in the guard's own output.

### 4. The census's THREE OWN defects, recorded first

| # | defect | caught by | consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | the declared archived/external/rext artifact names were never unioned into the derived set — `redis`, `clerk`, `directus`, `skiller` invisible to the proxy | **mutation control 11**, pre-publication | tier 2 undercounted by **229** (922 → 1,151) |
| 2 | a **bare basename** resolved by probing clone dirs alphabetically — a silent guess | **control `test_22b`**, post-dispatch | **41 of 525** quarantined; independently flagged by **6 of 12** adjudicators |
| 3 | materialization read the clones' **WORKING TREES**; 6 of 13 are behind their own fetched `origin/main` | **the adjudicators**, not a control | **2 false `DOES-NOT-SUPPORT`** verdicts |

Defect 3 is the sharpest thing this iter learned. `storage` is **20** commits behind, `messenger` 7,
`jobsimulation` 4, `next-web-app` 4, `cms` 2, rext 1. Four independent adjudicators booked the corpus's
M810 claim as contradicted — and **the corpus is right**: `6092c6d2` **is an ancestor of `origin/main`
`82cb66ec` in the very clone the census read from**.

> **A stale substrate does not merely fail to confirm a claim — it manufactures evidence AGAINST a true
> one.** The guard cannot fix the checkout (the clone set belongs to a live demo stack this milestone may
> not touch), so it discloses: substrate on every materialization, a staleness table on every run, and
> `KNOWN_WEAKNESS` clause (5) naming the failure mode in the guard's own output.

### 5. `F1` — the blind recall audit does not fire

60 prose lines drawn with **seed 122 and sealed in the pre-registration commit, before the enumerator
existed**, adjudicated blind. Auditor booked **36 ASSERTION**; the census placed 24 in tier 1, 10 in
tier 2, and **2 in neither** → **enumeration recall 34/36 = 94.4 %** against a **≥ 90 %** floor; miss
rate 5.6 % against a **> 10 %** firing threshold.

Both misses are `UNCITED PLAIN`, which **sharpens** the FLOOR disclosure: **tier-2 recall against the
auditor's own uncited class is 10/13 = 76.9 %.** Precision (measured, not pre-registered): 1 of 24
NOT-ASSERTION rows booked = 4.2 %.

### 6. The whole-suite run found two more provenance defects — in the fence this very iter shipped

Third occurrence of one class, and this time inside the iter that argues for whole-suite runs:
`claim_census_guard` **stamped no tree at all** on direct execution, and its `--json` printed a bare
document followed by the text summary on the same stream — **unparseable stdout**, the exact regression
`fence_provenance` exists to fence. Green under every scoped run this iter took. Fixed on both halves;
the ratchet verdict now rides *inside* the document. A disclosure regression was caught while fixing it:
`KNOWN_WEAKNESS` was gated on `--census`, which is **not the verb `guard_family` uses** — a guard whose
qualifier is invisible in the family run is a guard whose green over-claims.

### 7. What the census establishes that a reading cannot

1. **A denominator** — 2,603 cited claims / 2,485 claiming units / 3,292 assertion candidates. Every `P`
   this milestone published was a numerator with nothing behind it.
2. **That the milestone's own published error rates were sample artifacts**, by about nineteen-fold.
3. **Exhaustive coverage of a named class** — 525 of 525, with the 41 it could not resolve **named**
   rather than guessed.
4. **A defect class no fence could see** — 1,151 unevidenced assertions, hedge discipline at 24/1,175.
5. **A ratchet** — the per-file count cannot rise without a guard going RED. The only mechanism in this
   milestone that acts on tier 2 at all.

**What it does NOT establish:** that the corpus is correct (a `SUPPORTS` verdict is about the citation,
not the world); that the residual pool is small (**1,908** tier-1 pairs unadjudicated, **1,151** tier-2
assertions unverified — the floor is now *larger* and *better named*); and **nothing whatever about
clause 5**.

Caller-supplied scope always wins; both branches exercised end-to-end. `SKILL.md` gained the scope note **and** the `STACK_PROJECT`/`STACK_OFFSET` requirement — unset, the probes silently target the main dev stack. Fence: `stack-verify/tests/test_probe_scope_m257x.py` (**6 tests**), load-bearing arm **derived-vs-declared in both directions** — the registry rows the platform no longer declares, minus the rows this tooling itself injects, must **equal** the set the disclosure names — so the warning goes RED at the next fold instead of reassuring a reader about the wrong four services (`D-M257x-148-2`; a hand-written set is what rotted as six count literals at iter-145). ⚠️ **THE RED-PROOF CAUGHT A REAL DEFECT IN THIS ITER'S OWN FENCE** (`D-M257x-148-3`): the "derivation precedes invocation" check used bare `src.find()` and **both tokens bound to COMMENTS** — `generate.sh`'s usage header names `verify.sh`, and the comment block explaining this very fix names `platform_topology.py` — so it would have kept passing after the guarded code was deleted. **The mutation control found it, not review.** Now bound to executable content only. `§5` rule 67/68(d)'s axis, reproduced inside the fence written to apply it, **twice in one iter**. Gates: fence **6 passed** with the RED-proof proven load-bearing; `stack-verify` **244 passed · 0 failed** (238 at iter-145's close + 6 new); guard family **19 GREEN · 0 RED · 4 not-run**, identical to iter-147's close. `stack-core`/`demo-stack`/`dev-stack`/`stack-injection` **not re-run and saying so** (rule 60) — zero files touched in them, and two of the four ran in full one iter ago. **No `N` reading taken, so no `N` movement is claimed.** Gate **4 of 5**, unchanged — see [`iter-148/progress.md`](iter-148/progress.md)
- iter-149 (tik): **the emitter census widened from one retired service to all twelve — 354 references, 17 in executable position, 0 emitters** — closing iter-146's route with a falsification rather than a repair. The defect the census found was **in the census's own denominator**: the raw run returned 50 executable-content hits, of which **33 (66 %) were `.m220-mutant-*` files** — copies the M220 mutation battery stages *beside* its subjects (deliberately: those scripts resolve siblings from `$HERE` and cannot run from `/tmp`) and deletes in `tearDown`, left behind by interrupted runs and **accumulating since 2026-08-04**. Every one carried, verbatim, the tailnet-URL line iter-146 repaired — **a stale mutant is a perfect forgery of the bug its own battery exists to catch**, and `.gitignore:32` had described the leak in as many words the whole time (iter-148's lesson 0 again: a file that discloses a hazard in a comment is a file nobody fixed). Repaired by a `setUpClass` sweep that is **age-gated at 1 h, never unconditional** — a mutant belongs to a live run while that run holds it, and deleting a concurrent sibling's staging surfaces as a *mutation result*, not as a missing file; proven on the real leftovers before the fixture was spent, 33 → 0 repo-wide. Also a near-miss worth keeping: the pytest run failed under homebrew `python3` and the first draft booked a route for it — **`§9`'s first measurement-precondition bullet already said the interpreter is `/usr/bin/python3`**, and reading it deleted a fabricated finding. `§9` gains the exhaust-vs-subject precondition and a new subsection, *A census that returns ZERO must prove its instrument*. — see iter-149/progress.md
- iter-150 (tik): **the constants that claim a derivation nobody performs — 960 censused by parse, 30 keyword hits, 9 self-claims, 1 defect.** Opened by iter-149's own route hours earlier. The 30 → 9 → 1 narrowing IS the finding: the word *derive* appears in a constant's comment for at least four unrelated reasons (something else derives FROM it — all nine `FENCE_KIND` declarations; it is a fixture for a derivation under test; the comment discusses derivation as the choice being rejected; the sentence is about a neighbouring value), so a token count would have reported **30 defects where there is 1**. It already checked one direction (a blocking name no grading uses, which would make it green for free) and never the other: **a field the protocol grades that neither tuple classifies is treated as non-blocking BY OMISSION, indistinguishable in the output from a decision that it is safe.** Not hypothetical — `budget-exhausted` entered the exit enum on 2026-08-06 (three sessions had reported a clean budget stop, the enum had no value for it, each was told to emit `user-blocker` and each was flagged as a mis-grade) and someone hand-added it here. The repair splits the claim: **the partition stays DECLARED** (which side a condition falls on is a judgement no parse can make, and pretending otherwise is what produced the false claim) **and its COMPLETENESS is DERIVED** from the gradings the guard already parses — reported as a finding naming the first iter that graded the unknown field, not as a `could-not-run` that would suppress the blocking gradings the guard exists to surface. Paired controls: a fixture grading a net-new `(8) host-quarantine: y` yields exactly one finding; the real seven-field grading yields none. One existing mutation control needed narrowing (a second mechanism now writes to the same findings list) — scoped at the assertion with the reason in-line, never silently. 19/19 green; live run across all 149 graded iters: `OK`, **zero partition findings**, so the seven classified fields are exactly the seven M257x has ever graded — derived, not assumed. `§9`'s new subsection gains the completeness corollary. — see iter-150/progress.md
- iter-151 (tik): **the last open arm of the absent-value class closes with a falsification.** iter-147 booked `STACK_PROJECT` / `STACK_OFFSET` as an open hazard on the SHAPE of the default alone — unset, `STACK_PROJECT` resolves to `anthropos`, the developer's own main dev stack, and `STACK_OFFSET` derives from it. **The census that settles it is not "where is this read" but "is any reader a WRITER"**: every non-comment, non-prose read in the monorepo is inside `stack-verify/`, a read-only probe section, and `autoverify.sh` exports the variable explicitly before delegating. **Zero reads in stack-seeding, stack-snapshot, stack-injection, stack-secrets, demo-stack, dev-stack, playthroughs, clerkenstein or alignment.** So the live failure mode is a probe pointed at the wrong stack — iter-148's false-report class, loud and recoverable — and NOT a seeder or replay writing into a developer's own stack because a variable was missing from an environment. the symptom, `npm error code ENOENT`, names neither file nor reason and lands in a *different section* — iter-145's `12 != 13` again. ⚠ **D2, pre-existing, side-deliverable:** `blocking_state_guard` located the grading with an **unanchored `search()`**, so `iter-150` — **the iter that DOCUMENTED that guard** — had its own line-30 prose (*"the guard that reads every iter's `**Phase 5 grading:**` line"*) parsed as its grading, 68 lines early, swallowing a documented **fixture** `(8) host-quarantine: y`. **Both of the guard's live RED findings were that one phantom**, and `deferrals-audit.md`'s *"zero open user questions"* banner was **correct all along**. `^`-anchored + last-match-wins (**both halves controlled — anchoring alone is defeated by an iter quoting a prior grading at column 0**); Fence: `test_scope_union_m257x.py` (**16 tests**, every class **above** the `__main__` guard — pass 35 appended two below its own and a direct run collected 6 of 16 printing OK; this one collects 16 both ways, verified). **The answer key is GENERATED, never written down** — every expectation comes from running the real generator against a real clone at test time, because a fence that hard-codes the tuple it checks re-creates the defect one layer up; no platform clone ⇒ SKIP with the reason named, never a literal fallback. Arms: strict-subset · the flag matrix (carrying an **inversion instruction** so closing the routed bring-up fix cannot silently delete it) · **partition completeness** · **two independent parsers** (shell awk vs python — one parser on both sides would hide its own bug) · declaration-vs-measurement **both directions** · the disclosure **executed, not grepped**. 6 controls run: 2 mutation (the second exists because the first alone would not prove the arm that matters), 3 anti-vacuity, 1 subjects-exist. **RED-PROOF against the REAL pre-fix code from `HEAD`: 4 of 16 fail** — the other 12 are regression pins, **not** gap proofs, and are booked as such. ⚠️ **The repair obsoleted pass 35's own fence and it was RE-POINTED, not deleted** (`D-M257x-153-4`): it asserted `generate.sh`'s *source* carries three service-name literals, which the repair deliberately removed (a hand-written exclusion list is iter-145/iter-147's class); deleting it would retire a real property along with an obsolete spelling of it — the exact shape pass 35 booked against itself. - iter-155 (tik): **the repair was built, measured working, and then REFUSED by a pinned design decision it would have reversed on partly-unobservable grounds — reverted in full, and the argument is the deliverable.** `TOK-08`. First, `SURVEY-M257x-iter154-other-fences-may-be-pinned-to-spellings` was **sized and rejected with the numbers recorded**: **2,854** string-literal `assertIn`/`assertNotIn` across 109 files in 5 sections, **766** expression-shaped under the naive predicate — iter-150's 30-to-1 over-report at 30× the volume, so **the work is a sharper predicate, not the sweep**. Took `FIX-M257x-iter153-stack-injected-services-have-no-rows` instead. **`D-M257x-155-1`: iter-153's blocker was a CONJUNCTION whose conjuncts have different provenances** — *"a container, a port and a health target, observed on a live stack"* binds two **derivable** ingredients to one that is not (container name: the layout constant already owns it; base ports: `gen_injected_override.py` at offset 0 gives `hiring-app 3001 / fake-fapi 5400 / fake-bapi 5401`; health target: genuinely unobservable here). The registry's **`docker` kind needs only a container name**, so all three can carry a row that asserts something true without inventing a target — and `docker` **is** the "liveness-only" the prior decision itself asked for. Built and **measured working**: union returned all 6 extras probeable / 0 unprobeable, `service_registry_guard` **ALIGNED — 15 rows (7 graded, 8 declared absent)**, a 10-test fence green, **RED-proofed 4 of 10** against pre-fix `services.sh`. ⚠️ **Then Phase D returned 9 failed, and one was not a stale fence:** `test_fake_fapi_and_fake_bapi_have_NO_services_sh_row_by_design` — *"pinned so a future edit has to argue with it."* **Both of its rationales were investigated and both are answerable** — *"a row is emitted for EVERY project"* is now handled by the scope derivation **iters 153–154 built** (which did not exist when the decision was made, and which three existing rows already rely on), and *"neither fits the probe kinds"* is answered by `docker`; **and the gap is real and measured**: `/test-platform` runs `verify.sh`, **not** `autoverify.sh` (`generate.sh:166`), so the container-liveness block the pinned decision relies on **is never reached on the report path**. **The argument is strong and was still not made here** — reversing it rests partly on live-stack facts (the fake FAPI's TLS behaviour, the fake BAPI's loopback publish) this session cannot observe, and landing it meant re-pointing three more count fences at the end of a budget. **A half-landed reversal of a pinned decision is worse than either leaving it or doing it properly.** Reverted in full; routed as `FIX-M257x-iter155-add-injected-rows-vs-the-pinned-liveness-only-decision` with every measurement attached (~20 min to land next session, an iter to re-derive). ⚠️ **Side-deliverables — the THIRD and FOURTH instances of `§5` rule 71 in three iters, and the third is the sharp one: it was written by iter-154, THE ITER THAT AUTHORED RULE 71, and failed it one iter later.** `test_bringup_verify_scope_m257x.py` asserted as literals that the three services are *not* in scope and that the tail prints `UNGRADED`; iter-155's row addition made both false **while the code got more correct**. - iter-161 (tik): **the frozen-expectation class is RUN TO ZERO and ratcheted — `TOK-08`'s third clause, the one that turns a census into a fence.** All 10 candidates graded **at source**; **8 repaired by derivation** (`platform_topology.default_services(PLATFORM)`, bound once as `DEFAULT_SERVICES`/`PLAT` — the file already required a platform clone, so no new precondition), **2 declared exempt with reasons**: `0 unexempted (2 exempt) over 9,403 multi-token literals in 108 test files, against 3 executed derivations`. ⚠️ **`D-M257x-161-1`: this iter's OWN overview reading was refuted at source** — it graded `test_platform_predicate_guard.py:193` as a literal asserted *against the live platform*, and declared that reading a hypothesis (iter-158's rule); at source it is `self.platform = write_platform(self.root)`, **a SYNTHETIC compose the test writes into a tmpdir**. **A golden over a synthetic input is not a frozen copy of a live derivation** — the first is the test's whole content, and deriving it would assert that the derivation agrees with itself. **Measured precision cost: 2 of 10 (20 %) are that shape** — the instrument compares VALUES and cannot know the **provenance of the input**, and a good fixture deliberately mirrors the real platform. Stated as a limit with an exemption each, routed rather than papered over with a heuristic. ⚠️ **`D-M257x-161-2` is the finding, and it came from the direction nobody watches: THE SUCCESSFUL REPAIR DESTROYED THE PROOF THAT THE INSTRUMENT WORKS.** iter-160's labeled set read the **working tree** (iter-155's instance was live there); repairing the 8 sites made the proof return `MISMATCH — a prediction failed`. Nothing was wrong with the instrument; the **subject** had changed. **Both obvious fixes — delete the label, or flip its expectation to BLIND — would have left a census reporting ZERO with no surviving demonstration that it can fire at all**: `§9`'s unfalsifiable-instrument failure reached not through a bad guard but through a *good repair*. **A labeled set that reads the working tree decays the moment you use it.** Every instance now names the commit that carries it: **`@4adc595` FIRES at 8 sites, `@HEAD` silent** — simultaneously the proof and the ratchet's RED-proof, one instrument on two refs. **This milestone has audited every guard for *can it fire*; it had never audited one for *will it still be able to show that once the tree is clean*.** **`D-M257x-161-3`: the exemption-marker window (3 lines) bit immediately** — a marker at the top of a five-line comment silently did not take. **The window is NOT widened**: one wide enough to be convenient is wide enough for an exemption to drift onto a later, different assertion and excuse it silently — manufacturing a false green in the very file where someone was being careful. - iter-171 (tik): **the last unexplained runner disagreement was a shipped defect, not a harness artifact.** iter-170 left one RED it could not attribute to imports — two `test_cockpit` bind tests failing under 3.14 and passing under 3.9.6. One `sys._current_frames()` dump of the blocked thread named it outright: CPython's `http.server.HTTPServer.server_bind` sets `server_name = socket.getfqdn(host)` — a synchronous **reverse-DNS query**, on the critical path of every bind, whose result **nothing reads** (it fills the CGI `SERVER_NAME`; the cockpit builds no CGI environment). Same Mac, same address: **35.005 s cold on homebrew 3.14.6 vs 0.005 s on Apple 3.9.6**, warm ~0 s on both — and the two do not even agree on the answer. The runner split is fully explained by *bind order*: unittest loads classes alphabetically so `TestContentTabMainWiring` is the process's first binder and eats 35 s inside a 2 s window; pytest collects in definition order so `TestMainServes` absorbs it 2000 lines earlier and every later test finds the resolver warm. **The production consequence is a false negative, not a delay** — `up-injected.sh` polls the cockpit's `/healthz` 25 × 0.2 s ≈ **5 s**, so a 35 s bind prints *"presenter cockpit FAILED to come up … there is NO working cockpit"* **and skips fronting it with `tailscale serve`** (that call sits inside the `cockpit_ok=1` branch), leaving a `--public-host` demo's single entry point on plain HTTP while it quietly starts serving half a minute later. Censused as a class across **all of `rosetta-extensions`**, denominator stated: **4** `.py` files mention `HTTPServer`, carrying **3** reachable server classes and **13** construction sites — **13 of 13 were paying it**, and **0** `server_bind` overrides existed anywhere. **The tests were part of the population, not observers of it:** 12 of the 13 sites imported the class from `http.server`, so a green suite was exercising a bind path production does not run. Repaired with CPython's own `server_bind` minus one call (`server_name`/`server_port` still populated — asserted, per iter-158). **The fence RED-ed on its author's first attempt and was right:** the first repair kept the stock class as a module-level alias, the population check flagged it, and carving it out by its leading underscore was **rejected** under `§5` rules 70/71 (a fence pinned to a spelling is not pinned to a property) — the alias was deleted, so the fence keeps **zero exemptions**. Fenced by **poisoning `socket.getfqdn`** rather than timing the bind, for three independent reasons (the OS cache makes every warm reading zero — which is how this survived 171 iters; rule 51's timing leg is unusable on this host; a slow stub would cost the suite the seconds the iter reclaims), with CPython's own class as the mutation control and a fail-closed prefilter/reach floor as anti-vacuity — both shown firing. Re-measured with the runner named: `demo-stack` 35 modules, **unittest 31 GREEN / 4 ENV-GATED / 0 RED**, **pytest 31 / 4 / 0**, **runner disagreement 1 → 0**. Booked as `§5` rule 76 and as the **fourth arithmetic signature** in `latency-budget.md` (*one process pays it, every later call is ~0, and it lands before the surface exists*). NEW route: the two runners execute **1073 vs 1062** tests over the same 35 modules — 11 tests with an unstated scope (**✅ CORRECTED at iter-172: the reading was refuted — not one test executes under only one runner; the census column carried two units and all 11 decompose as 9 ENV-GATED failures + 2 skips**). **A second finding fell out of the repair itself:** the 43-line insert moved **6** corpus pins into `cockpit.py`, the pre-commit anchor guard caught 2 — and **2 of the 6 were already wrong before this iter** (`latency-budget.md:44`'s `:1214`/`:882`, off by ~160 and ~150 lines), having survived every prior run because they landed on a docstring tail and a live JS statement rather than on a blank line or a closing delimiter. The guard fired only because the new offset pushed one onto a `}`. **A pin that rots onto plausible-looking code is invisible to it** — `SURVEY-M257x-iter163-anchors-with-no-quoted-literal` measured rather than argued. All 6 repaired. — see iter-171/progress.md
- iter-172 (tik): **the milestone's own census counted two different things and printed them in one column — and iter-171's reading of the gap is REFUTED.** iter-171 published *"1073 vs 1062 — 11 tests execute under one runner and not the other, and nobody has named which."* Decomposed per module, the 11 resolve with **zero residue**: **9 ENV-GATED failures + 2 skips**, and **not one test executes under only one runner**. Root cause is four characters wide: `suite_census.run_one` derived the `tests` figure from `^Ran (\d+) test` under unittest (passes + failures + errors + **skips**) and from `(\d+) passed` under pytest (**passes only**) — two different denoted sets, printed side by side under the banner *"runners DISAGREE about N module(s)"*, a report whose entire purpose is cross-runner comparison. `test_migrate_race_live` was the tell nobody read: **3 → 0**, a module reported as having *no tests* by a census whose job is to enumerate tests. **It survived 35 modules because the two units coincide on any all-passing, skip-free module — 29 of 35** (a defect invisible on the healthy majority is found by the sick minority or not at all). Repaired toward the **larger, honest set** (`pytest_executed()` sums passed+failed+error+skipped+xfailed+xpassed from the LAST summary-bearing line): `deselected` is deliberately excluded (it never ran, and `Ran N` would not have counted it — summing it would make the columns agree by inflating one, the flattering fix `§5` refuses), and the last-line rule stops pytest's per-test `FAILED …` block being double-counted. Fenced with a fixture of **one pass, one failure, one skip** — the minimal input on which the two units MUST disagree — plus a mutation control that pins the **separation** (the old parse must still read `1` on it), so the fence cannot go vacuous on a degenerate all-green input; `+5` tests, green on both interpreters. Re-measured: `demo-stack` **1073 = 1073**, modules with a count difference **6 → 0**. Whole population re-measured at the corrected unit — 112 modules, **unittest 104 GREEN / 4 ENV-GATED / 4 RED / 3311 tests**, **pytest 105 / 4 / 3 / 3350** — with the arithmetic `3,332 → 3,350` **explicitly refused** as a measure of the unit error (the population grew by 2 modules in the same window); the clean figure is the fixed-population slice, **+11 of 1073 = 1.02 %**. The residual whole-population gap of **39 IS a population fact** now that the artifact is gone: the three modules the runners genuinely disagree about. **And the whole-population run caught a regression this iter had just introduced** — the new census tests were appended with `cat >>` and landed *after* the module's `if __name__ == "__main__"` block, turning `test_test_collection_fence` RED under both runners; repaired, 27 tests OK on both. That is `FIX-M257x-iter142-whole-suite-owed` firing in its useful direction for the first time: the change-derived scoped run was green and structurally could not see the fence that grades it. NEW routes: 2 pre-existing ACTIONABLE REDs (verified by reading their failures, not assumed), and every pytest test-count this milestone published before today is a *passed* count. — see iter-172/progress.md
- iter-173 (tik): **the milestone's own rule that a whole-suite figure must name its denominator was published WITH A WRONG DENOMINATOR — and the arithmetic had been quoted four times without once being re-added.** `TOK-08`, working the derived half of `SURVEY-M257x-iter172-published-counts-predate-the-unit-fix`. iter-172 routed *"every pytest count published before this iter is a `passed` count, therefore an undercount"* — a class spanning both a censusable half and an unaffordable one, so this iter **split it by DERIVABILITY** (`D-M257x-173-1`): an **observed** count (`1 failed · 1229 passed`, read off a runner) cannot be re-checked without re-running at a ref that may not exist — `§5` rule 51's timing leg is unusable here and iter-172's two-runner census cost 50 minutes — while a **derived** count (a total over a table, a delta over its endpoints, a percentage over its own operands) re-derives **on the page, in under a second**. a clause that fails = RED with the symmetric difference printed) by `ARationaleThatAssertsASetRelationIsGRADED`. **RED-proven against the UNREPAIRED tree first** — both arms failed naming the two entries — because a fence first seen green over a repaired tree is a fence nobody has watched fail (`§9` iter-149). **The pre-registered escalation was checked and did NOT fire:** the sealed condition was *"if the resolver needs a per-site lookup table, keep the prose repair only"*, and one generic resolver (`module::attr`; call if callable; flatten to a set of `str`) covers all **five** operands including `repair_postcondition::discover_fences`, which returns a **pair** of lists — no lookup table, so no new registry, so no new tax (the flatten's control is written against the QUIET direction: taking only the first half would report a plausible 6-vs-26 disagreement rather than raising). Also measured and **deliberately not settled**: `declaring_modules` and the REGISTERED `discover_fences` return the **identical** set, so `SURVEY-M257x-iter175-census-vs-discover_fences-classified-differently` now rests on a measurement instead of on the comment just shown to be wrong about its neighbour. - iter-181 (tik): **a coverage question has no answer until it names its denominator — and the obvious denominator is usually the wrong one.** `TOK-08`, closing `SURVEY-M257x-iter179-readme-indexes-test-modules-unmeasured` **by refuting the denominator it implied**. The survey asked how completely `stack-core/README.md` indexes **test modules**; measured at HEAD both ways: **10 of 63** over all `tests/test_*.py`, and **6 of 7** over mutation batteries. **`10 of 63` would have been a published defect, not a finding** — the index's subject is the fence family and its batteries, and the other 53 are per-guard *behaviour* suites the index deliberately does not list (it lists the guard); answering on the question's own terms would have put an **84 % gap that does not exist** into a corpus whose whole quarrel is with unstated denominators (`§9` iter-159 turned on a **question** rather than on a fence). At the answerable denominator the gap was **real**: `test_repair_leak_guard_mutation_battery.py` — 20 mutants, 19 kills, the only proof the *"did this commit FINISH?"* fence can fail — shipped at **iter-48** (`932554e`, 2026-08-02) and was **unindexed for 133 iters**. Nobody misread anything; **there was no denominator, so there was nothing to be short of.** Row added, and the arm that would have said so ships with it, **RED-proven against the unrepaired tree first**, naming the file. Also landed: the **other direction** (the index names no battery that is gone) and a **repo-wide resolve arm** — whose *naive* form was written first, scoped to `stack-core/`, and **reported `exposure_claim_guard.py` missing when it simply lives in `stack-injection/` and is cross-referenced in the README's own note**; the discarded version is **kept as a mutation control** that goes RED on purpose if that cross-section reference ever disappears, so the narrowing must be re-derived rather than silently adopted (*a fence scoped more narrowly than its subject manufactures findings* — the instrument-side form of `D-M257x-122-4`). All four arms went into the module that already owns the README's disclosed limit — **no new module, no new registry tax, third consecutive iter** (iter-178's lesson). **Deliberately NOT done:** `repair_leak_guard.py` (the *guard*) also has no row, but that is 1 of the 11 members of `SURVEY-M257x-iter175-readme-fence-index-is-16-of-27` and repairing it would move a **published, fenced triple** while answering none of that survey's question; and the observed half of `SURVEY-M257x-iter172-published-counts-predate-the-unit-fix` was probed first and **set aside WITH a number** — **412 count-mentions across 115 files**, defective subset not regex-decidable (*a NOT-REACHED clause is a measurement or it is a mood*, iter-178, applied to a routing decision). Runs: **16 passed** in the arms' module (was 11), **119 passed · 0 failed** over the registry/census/guard-family scope (22.77 s), `guard_family` **18 GREEN · 0 RED**; pytest 8.4.2 / 3.9.6. - iter-182 (tik): **the third way a test file hides its tests — it needs a RUNNER.** `TOK-08`, half-closing `FIX-M257x-iter170-two-modules-cannot-run-on-the-modern-interpreter` and **re-homing the other half inside a running fence**. The route named two modules as a pair; **sized first, they are not one job** — `test_gen_override_home_binds.py` is 165 lines / **16** tests / one `@fixture` + one `@parametrize` + one `monkeypatch`, while `test_claim_census_guard.py` is 481 lines / **25** tests / `@fixture` **plus** `tmp_path`, `monkeypatch` and `capsys`, i.e. three builtin shims. *A route that names N things has not established that they are one piece of work* (`D-M257x-182-1`). The tractable one is **converted assertion-for-assertion**: every `assert`, message and docstring unchanged, the fixture a plain builder called on each test's first line, `monkeypatch.setattr` an explicit save/restore in `try/finally`. **The real risk was silent NARROWING, not correctness:** the obvious `subTest` translation of a 5-case `@parametrize` still runs every assertion and still reports every failure — and collects **12 instead of 16**, a count moving during a change whose whole contract is that nothing moves, which would have been absorbed into a suite total three iters later. So five **named methods**, and the count proven on both interpreters: **16 collected → 16 passed** under pytest 8.4.2 / `/usr/bin/python3` 3.9.6, and **could not run at all → `Ran 16 … OK`** under unittest / `/opt/homebrew/bin/python3` 3.14.6 (`D-M257x-182-2`). **The class is then closed by a derived enumeration rather than by the conversion:** population repo-wide is now **1**, keyed on the **DECORATOR and not the import** — `pytest.skip()` is a call inside a test body and runs fine under `unittest`, so a predicate keyed on `import pytest` would have read as a fleet-wide crisis instead of one open member, and a control keeps that distinction load-bearing (`D-M257x-182-3`). The arms went into **`test_test_collection_fence.py`**, whose subject — *"a test file may not hide tests from the runner that reads it"* — already carried the other two ways (statement ORDER, failure to IMPORT); **no new module, no new registry tax, fourth consecutive iter**. Shipped: the classified population **both directions** (unclassified = RED, stale blocker = RED), a **ratchet** stating its floor (2 at iter-170 → **1** here), a regression arm on the converted module, and the decorator-vs-import control — **all three assertion arms RED-proven LIVE** against the real population (`D-M257x-182-4`). Runs: **20 passed** in the fence module (was 16); **1,535 passed · 2 skipped · 0 failed** over `stack-core` minus all 7 batteries (455.54 s) — **checkable arithmetic: iter-179's 1,521 + 5 + 5 + 4 + 0 = 1,535**, and with the batteries' 41, `stack-core` is **1,576** collected. Not covered: the 7 batteries (neither touched module is staged by any) and the four other rext sections. **No `P`/`N` reading taken — UNMEASURED, not unmoved.** Gate **4 of 5**, unchanged. → §8 gains *a test file hides its tests THREE ways, and the third is that it needs a runner* — see [`iter-182/progress.md`](iter-182/progress.md)
- iter-183 (tik): **an instrument's own BACKLOG is a registry, and it rots exactly like the ones it watches.** `TOK-08`. M257x has fences over the corpus index, `repos.yml`, the demo knobs, the anchors, the retractions, the derived counts, the guard family and the guards' own tests — and **none** over the **carry-forward route queue**, the artifact that decides what the next iter works on and the substrate every sub-agent brief is assembled from: **183 routes · 779 dispositions · 164 close-blocks · 0 assertions.** The consumer had already noticed from outside — run 17's brief listed a route as open with the hedge *"may already be closed — verify before working it"* (it was closed at iter-180). One layer deeper sat the real one: **`SURVEY-M257x-iter170-cockpit-runner-dependence`, CLOSED BY ROOT CAUSE at iter-171** (`D-M257x-171-1`, a blocking `socket.getfqdn` in shipped `cockpit.py` — 35.005 s on the 3.14 interpreter against a 2 s bind window) and **re-published as *"unchanged; open"* by iter-182**, eleven iters later, describing the mechanism iter-171 had already attributed and repaired. **A stale carry-forward and a legitimate re-open are the same sentence**, so the verdict was settled by re-running the subject rather than reading the record: **`207 = 207 = 207`**, `unittest`/3.9.6 · `unittest`/3.14.6 · `pytest`/3.9.6, zero failures each — a **correction**, landed as an in-place `✅ CORRECTED — iter-183` annotation, never a rewrite of a closed record. **The grammar is the deliverable.** A first detector binding a verdict to the whole BULLET returned **6**; adjudicated one at a time, **1 is true and 5 are false**, each for a nameable reason — three multi-id bullets whose `CLOSED` belongs to a neighbour, one **TABLE** row whose verdict is *superseded*, one **half** closure iter-174 correctly scoped to *"the observed half"*. Booking 6 would have been `D-M257x-122-3` in miniature. A detector rewritten to the tokens the registry **already writes** (` · ` segments · `half CLOSED` · *supersedes* · tables-are-not-bullets) returns exactly **1**, matching the hand adjudication independently. Shipped **`route_disposition_guard.py`** + **20 controls** (`unittest` only — **20/20 under BOTH runners**), in `guard_family`'s `INVOCATIONS`, stamped, README-indexed. **Its own docstring's first draft claimed the registry never writes two ids in one segment; measured, 28 do and 2 carry a verdict** — so the guard applies `D-M257x-122-5` at segment grain (**REFUSED, never resolved by proximity**) and **prints the refusal count every run**. The **reading hazard underneath everything**: these ids hard-wrap mid-token, so a line-scoped reader returns **207 ids where 204 exist** — 3 phantoms, 3 real ids invisible; `_unwrap` rejoins and **counts the joins** so the hazard stays demonstrated. Runs: **1,594 passed · 2 failed · 2 skipped** over `stack-core` (pytest 8.4.2 / 3.9.6, 1,312.71 s) — **both failures were this fence's own registration debt** (`test_fence_provenance`: it did not stamp its tree; `test_fence_registry_population_m257x`: the README disclosure is a **measurement** and moved, `16/27 · 16/26 · 15/26` → **`17/28 · 17/27 · 16/27`**), both repaired → **54/54 green**, and the collision the move creates is named in place (**`16 of 27` now denotes a `union` reading at `5b108d0` AND a `declaring` reading at HEAD**). Not covered: the four other rext sections. **No `P`/`N` reading taken — UNMEASURED, not unmoved.** Gate **4 of 5**, unchanged. → §8 gains *an instrument's own BACKLOG is a registry* — see [`iter-183/progress.md`](iter-183/progress.md)
- iter-184 (tik): **a fence's POPULATION is a registry too, and it is the one nobody audits.** `TOK-08`, working the second mechanical property of the population iter-183 enumerated — and finding that the population itself was wrong. Started from iter-183's own routed residual: a member of the fence's population that is **not a route** — `SURVEY-M257x-iter181-`, the stem left by iter-182's `` `SURVEY-M257x-iter181-*` ``. Measured: **iter-181 created ZERO routes** (its close names seven ids, every one pre-existing), so **the glob denotes ∅** while reading as a live backlog item in every brief quoting this queue, run 17's included. `§5` rule 73 said *a glob is not a derivation*; what was missing was a **population to check it against**, which iter-183 had just built. **Repaired by WITHDRAWAL, not deletion** — deleting rewrites a closed iter's published record, so the glob is **struck** and the guard learns the retraction idiom the corpus already uses (`~~ai~~`, `~~authn~~`, `~~GraphQL/Cosmo Router~~`; this registry used it at iter-29), with two safeguards because a stripper that hides a live route is this fence's own failure mode: the struck-span count **prints every run**, and the pattern is deliberately **not `re.S`** (a DOTALL non-greedy span would let two unrelated `~~` marks swallow every id between them) — audited live, exactly **2** struck spans, withdrawing the glob and one iter-29 `CHECK-` id, **no live route**. **And that audit surfaced the real finding:** a `CHECK-` id the fence's grammar does not recognise **at all**. Censused inside the carry-forward blocks, **8 kinds are carried that iter-183's declared tuple excluded** — `CHECK` **76 distinct**, `DOC` 28, `FENCE` 15, `MEASURE` 10, `DEF` 3, `HOST`/`REPOINT`/`READ` 2 each — while **`PROBE` and `TASK`, 2 of its 5 declared kinds, never occur.** **189 of 327 — 57.8 %.** The fence shipped one iter earlier as *"the route registry"*, green, with green mutation **and** anti-vacuity controls and a printed denominator: **every control fired correctly, on the 57.8 %**, because a mutation battery perturbs the SUBJECT and the population decides what the subject is. This is `§2`'s hand-maintained tuple returning **inside the fence built to stop registries rotting**. **Fixed by REMOVING the tuple, not extending it** (iter-169: close the class, not its last member) — the kind is now derived, and the registry reads **312 routes · 1,156 dispositions · 36 closures · 0 contradictions** against iter-183's `184 · 789 · 33 · 0`; both correct about their own population, neither quotable without it (iter-177). **Widening cost nothing but honesty — no new contradiction.** It did immediately book `HOST-M257x-toolchain` as malformed, so the well-formedness predicate was narrowed to assert **exactly** the defect (*an id may not end in `-`*): **a predicate tuned to the majority shape manufactures findings about the minority shape**, visible only once the population is wide enough to contain one (`D-M257x-122-4`, instrument side). Runs: guard **EXIT 1 pre-repair naming the site**, **OK** post-repair; controls **25/25 under BOTH runners** (was 20); **124 passed · 0 failed** over the fence + both registry guards + the guard-family suite (126.77 s, pytest 8.4.2 / 3.9.6). **0 new `*_guard.py`**, so no `INVOCATIONS` entry, no new stamp, and the README index triple **does not move** — Phase 0d's prediction, checked rather than assumed. Not covered: the rest of `stack-core` (1,594 P at iter-183), the 7 batteries, the four other rext sections. **No `P`/`N` reading taken — UNMEASURED, not unmoved.** Gate **4 of 5**, unchanged. → §8 gains *a fence's POPULATION is a registry too* — see [`iter-184/progress.md`](iter-184/progress.md)
- iter-185 (tik): **try the DERIVATION first and record its refutation — then fence the declaration both ways.** `TOK-08`, testing whether iter-184's newly-named class (*a fence's POPULATION is a registry too*) has a second member. It does: `predicate_enumerator.CITATION_RE` declares **13 file extensions, typed**, and they decide what the enumerator can see at all. Measured over `corpus/**.md` + `CLAUDE.md` in file context, the corpus line-pins **11 more** — **`mod` 51**, `jsx` 20, `hcl` 6, `gitignore` 6, `example` 5, `ini` 4, `dev` 3, `txt` 2, plus `vue`/`css` found by the fence rather than the census. **The costly one is `mod`: `app/go.mod:14-18` alone is 12 citations** — the anchor CLAUDE.md's shared-libraries banner rests on, the claim about which org modules `app` actually requires — so any predicate anchored there sat **outside the reach denominator** `TOK-07`/iter-114 made a declared thing. **Derivation was attempted BEFORE the tuple was touched and is recorded with its verdict:** *a `/` in the stem means a path* → **REFUTED** (`.anthropos` has **14** with-slash hits, every one an `https://` authority); *not inside a `scheme://` token* → better, still leaves `api.clerk.com:443` and `backend.internal.anthropos:8083`; *resolve on disk* → ground truth but needs a clone set this enumerator does not take. So iter-184's **fallback clause** fires for the first time — the declaration becomes a registry with **both directions asserted**: *cited ⇒ declared* (RED, naming the extension with example tokens) and *declared ⇒ occurs* (RED — **the direction that let iter-184's own fence carry `PROBE` and `TASK`, two route kinds that had never existed**). **The arms found more than the census that motivated them**: the census used a **≥2 threshold** and a fence has none, so it named `vue`, `css` and `de` the moment it ran — *a threshold is a sampling decision and a fence is a census*; each was dispositioned individually (2 files; `de` = `u422950.your-storagebox.de:23`, the Hetzner Storage Box SSH port in `db-backup.md`, **named with its reason**, `§5` rule 8). The authority carve-out is **itself fenced** against becoming a blanket (no tail in both lists; the carve-out may not outgrow the class it carves out of) — *a reason list is how a blanket exclusion disguises itself*. **Reach delta PUBLISHED, not absorbed** (the iter's own pre-registered escalation condition): **1,276 → 1,376 citation tokens, +100, +7.8 %**, across **978 → 1,041** corpus lines carrying ≥1 citation — so any ratio previously computed against a corpus-derived citation denominator was computed over the smaller one, routed as `SURVEY-M257x-iter185-reach-percentages-predate-the-citation-widening`. Runs: **34/34 under BOTH runners** (was 29), arm **RED-proven pre-repair** naming 8 extensions then 3 more, **113 passed · 0 failed** over the touched scope + both registry guards + the iter-183/184 fence (119.82 s, pytest 8.4.2 / 3.9.6). **0 new `*_guard.py`**, so the README index triple does not move. Not covered: the rest of `stack-core` (1,594 P at iter-183), the 7 batteries, the four other rext sections. **No `P`/`N` reading taken — UNMEASURED, not unmoved.** Gate **4 of 5**, unchanged. → §8 gains *try the DERIVATION first and record its refutation* — see [`iter-185/progress.md`](iter-185/progress.md)
- iter-186 (tik): **a CORRECT exclusion is still a defect while it is silent — and the third member of a class is the one you should stop hand-sweeping for.** `TOK-08`. iters 184/185 each found a member of *a fence's POPULATION is a registry too* by judgement; iter-185 routed the honest gap (*nobody knows how many exist*), so this iter **enumerated** instead — **70 module-level string collections** in `stack-core` — and selected on **iter-177's shape used as a SEARCH KEY**: two literals naming one population with different cardinality. `claim_census_guard.REXT_SECTION_NAMES` says **11** (exactly the 11 non-`knowledge` dirs ✔); **`suite_census.SECTIONS` says 5.** `suite_census.py` is the instrument behind this milestone's **`3,369 passed · 9 failed · 4 skipped`** figure — quoted in run 17's brief and in iter closes as **"the whole-population baseline"** — and the six sections it omits are **not empty**: **264 `*_test.go` + 45 `*.spec.ts`** (`stack-seeding` **119**, `stack-snapshot` 45, `clerkenstein` 37, `playthroughs` 22 Go + 45 TS, `alignment` 21, `stack-secrets` 20). **Nothing about the omission is wrong** — no Python runner collects a Go test — **the silence is the defect**: `§5` rule 60 says a scoped green is evidence about its scope alone, and an instrument that does not PRINT its scope makes that rule unenforceable by the person reading the number. Repaired as a **derivation plus named reasons, never a widening**: `SECTIONS` derived from disk, the six excluded **by name with a reason each** (`§5` rule 8), and every run now prints `scope: 5 of 11 sections — Python only` **before any total**. Shipped `test_suite_census_population.py` — 4 arms + a derivation control (`derive_sections` must read the disk; the last-measured tuple reachable **only** through the cannot-locate-the-repo path, because *a silent fallback is how a derivation becomes the literal it replaced*), **2 mutants RED-proven with 3 failures each** (drop an exclusion → the section is invisible to both halves and the partition breaks; add an exclusion whose subject has no tests → the stale-exclusion + partition arms fire). **`D-M257x-145-3` is explicitly NOT ruled on** — that is the user's — but it now has the denominator it lacked: *"all five sections"* is **five of eleven**, so the assumption's own wording carried an unmeasured claim. Runs: **6/6 under BOTH runners**, **65 passed** over the touched modules (1.45 s), **84 passed · 0 failed** over `test_suite_census` + both registry guards (122.23 s), pytest 8.4.2 / 3.9.6. **0 new `*_guard.py`**, README index triple unmoved. Not covered — and now **stated rather than implied**: the **264 Go tests + 45 TS specs** in the six excluded sections, which **no reading in this milestone has ever included**, routed as `SURVEY-M257x-iter186-264-go-tests-have-never-been-read`. **No `P`/`N` reading taken — UNMEASURED, not unmoved.** Gate **4 of 5**, unchanged. → §8 gains *a CORRECT exclusion is still a defect while it is silent* — see [`iter-186/progress.md`](iter-186/progress.md)
- iter-187 (tik): **an exclusion registry sits at ONE grain; the runner's reach sits at another — and a membership check cannot see a hole INSIDE a member.** `TOK-08`. Run 17's brief named `SURVEY-M257x-iter185-other-declared-populations-unaudited`; **substituted deliberately** (`D-M257x-187-1`) because iter-186 recorded that its residual is *judgement* (*"there is no syntactic marker"*), and TOK-08 exists to stop paying for judgement where an enumeration exists. The enumeration here is one command — the **(section × language)** test-file matrix over 11 sections. iter-186 partitioned the repo 5 collected / 6 excluded-by-language, named every exclusion with a reason, printed the scope beside every total and mutation-proved four properties; **all of it correct, and all of it at SECTION grain.** Measured one grain down: **`stack-verify` is COLLECTED, carries 5 Python modules — so every membership arm is green over it, each one rightly — and ALSO carries 30 Playwright `*.spec.ts`** that no Python runner collects, that **no instrument in this milestone reads** (pre-registered escalation checked and did not fire — the only two `stack-core` files mentioning Playwright are a keyword in a vocabulary list and a citation fixture, `D-M257x-187-5`), and that the printed line `scope: 5 of 11 sections — Python only` presents as read. **iter-186's own rule recurring inside a member of the population iter-186 fixed.** Repaired as **declare-and-derive, never widen** (`D-M257x-187-2`; widening the census was considered and rejected — it changes what the tool DOES under cover of fixing what it SAYS): `UNREAD_IN_COLLECTED` declared by name+reason, `unread_non_python()` derived from disk, both directions fenced, and the scope block now prints the within-section remainder ahead of any total. **iter-186's `264 Go + 45 TS` is NOT retracted** — it is true and scoped to the six excluded sections at every site (`D-M257x-187-3`); what nothing published was a **repo-wide** figure, so the six-section one was the only number available to quote. Repo-wide: **264 Go + 75 TS**. Second finding, **latent and sized before repair** (`D-M257x-187-4`): harden pass 42's presence arm globbed `test_*.py` **and** `*_test.py` while the collector globs `test_*.py` **alone** — a **superset** predicate, so a section whose Python tests were all spelled `*_test.py` passes the arm and still contributes the silent zero the arm exists to prevent (`§5` r70/71, the defect re-entering through the spelling). **Hazard size measured, not asserted: 0 such files across all 11 sections.** Closed anyway by re-basing the arm on `S.modules()` plus a control that the collector still reads the named `PYTHON_TEST_GLOB`. Shipped **5 new arms + 1 control**, **6/6 mutants RED** (applied to the imported module so the tree is never edited mid-run — `D-M257x-187-6` states what that does and does not prove). Runs, each with runner and scope named (`§5` r60/75/76): **13/13** on the new module under **both** runners; **25 passed** with `test_suite_census`; **123 passed · 0 failed** over the 6-module registry/fence neighbourhood under pytest 8.4.2/3.9.6 (18.45 s); **98 passed · 0 failed · 1 module unloadable** under unittest 3.14.6 — the unloadable one is `test_claim_census_guard.py`'s `import pytest`, **pre-existing and already documented** in `suite_census.py`'s own header. **Side-effect of this iter's own close, dispositioned not suppressed:** citing `FIX-M257x-h36-labeled-prover-denominator` in the routes block made the backlog fence reach it and turned the harden-origin registry RED; the entry was **removed** rather than the citation dropped (`D-M257x-187-7`) — the route stays OPEN and Fate 3. **Not covered, stated rather than implied:** the other 5 `stack-core` Python modules were not re-run, and the **264 Go + 75 TS remain UNMEASURED** — this iter names 30 more of them than were nameable before and reads none. **No `P`/`N` reading taken — UNMEASURED, not unmoved.** Gate **4 of 5**, unchanged. → §8 gains *an exclusion registry sits at ONE grain; the runner's reach sits at another* — see [`iter-187/progress.md`](iter-187/progress.md)
- iter-188 (tik): **the citation resolver's own walk prunes 70 % of its candidate files, and said so nowhere.** `TOK-08`. iter-187 routed its residual **with a mechanical selector** — *a registry keyed by a CONTAINER whose justifying reason is a property of the container's CONTENTS* — and this iter **ran** it over `stack-core`'s 30 module-level registries instead of re-surveying by judgement (`D-M257x-188-1`; the class closing *by an enumeration that keeps running*). Strongest hit, and the one with the largest consequence: `claim_census_guard._SKIP_DIRS`, the 12-name prune list of the `os.walk` that builds the **basename index citations are resolved against** — a directory the walk never enters is a directory no citation can resolve into, so the list decides **reach**, not speed. **The iter's first environment reading was WRONG and is corrected in place** (`D-M257x-188-2`): `clones` falls back to `root/"stack-demo"` (`:768`), which exists here, so the DEFAULT run is the large one — **21,610 files indexed / 50,357 pruned / 70.0 %** (with `stack-demo` absent: 0 of 92). Of the twelve names **2 did all the pruning** (`node_modules` 3 dirs, `test-results` 2), **5 were inert**, and **5 can never fire at all** — the walk reads `d not in _SKIP_DIRS and not d.startswith(".")`, so `.git`/`.next`/`.venv`/`.turbo`/`.pnpm-store` are **subsumed by an adjacent unnamed rule** and the registry was over-stating its own work to anyone auditing it.
