Built the per-module **collection**-reach census — a different unit from the `tests=` column, declared as such — and read it: **123 modules · pytest 3,551 · unittest 3,526 · gap 25 · exactly 1 module DISAGREES**, the declared one at 25/0, corroborated independently by a static style census (**bare 1 · testcase 122**; unittest collects `TestCase` and nothing else). **5.9 s** — cost was never the reason it went unfenced. The instrument found **three defects in its own code**, each caught by a control written to prove it can fire: `collected_by_pytest` scraped `no tests collected, 1 error` into a **silent 0** (the `go_census` defect re-created within the hour; now read by pytest's exit-code contract), `collection_disagreements` scored **two blind runners as agreement** (`-1 != -1` is False), and a size literal rotted **inside its own iter** (*"the other 121 modules"* → 122) beside an arm that was an **identity**. The conversion itself is untouched: `FIX-M257x-h44-…` stays open with a correct size and a fence that fails the day a second member appears. — see iter-197/progress.md
- iter-198 (tik): `SURVEY-M257x-h46-…` said *declare the direction, or grade it* — **both, and both found something.** **Graded:** the STALE SUBSTRATE table warns over six behind-clones and reads as a caveat on the **949** tier-1 pairs materialized from the clone set; measured at cited-line grain it is **19 of 3,089** (20 sit on a drifted file, 1 of those cites lines that did not move, 0 cite a file absent at origin) — and **all 19 are terraform citations** in `jobsimulation` / `storage` / `messenger`, i.e. exactly the `service_desired_count` / M810 region this milestone was already wrong about at iter-122 and corrected at 123/124. **Declared:** clause (5) and `substrate_of` both asserted the **false-RED** direction as *the* consequence; the **false-GREEN** mirror is equally reachable and structurally *more* likely — corpus and clone fell behind **together**, so agreeing-and-both-wrong is the correlated case — retracted in place at both sites. And the verdict-scope sentence was **FALSE in its first draft** (*"tier 2 is computed from the corpus text and not from any clone's contents"* — `census` filters every tier-2 candidate through `_live_names`, which reads the clone dirs + `platform/repos.yml` + `docker-compose.yml`), replaced by a **derived** check over `NAME_SOURCE_FILES` that prints a ⚠ branch if a name-source file ever drifts. Exit code deliberately unchanged, with the reason recorded. Fence is offline/synthetic (`git init` + bare origin + `reset --hard HEAD~1`) so it does not go RED the day somebody updates the clones. — see iter-198/progress.md
- iter-199 (tik): the class harden pass 45 routed as *"fixed by hand, in one module"* had since produced a defect in **each of the two following work units** — pass 47's `2,714` under the wrong unit, and **iter-197's own** *"the other 121 modules"*, stale before its iter closed. **Three hand-repairs in three consecutive work units** is `TOK-08`'s signature, so it got a census: `printed_measurement_literals` over every non-test Python module — **11 hits across 62 modules in 4 sections: 2 `literal` (the defect), 2 `ordinal` (`TIER 1  pairs` — the digit names a tier), 7 `guarded-zero`** (`CANNOT RUN — 0 file(s)`, where the branch proves the value). It **classifies rather than filters**, and both excluded classes are asserted non-empty, because an exclusion nobody can size is iter-158's narrowing; the zero-exclusion is a **checkable condition** on the message (a bare `OK — 0 files` stays a finding), measured 7 of 7. Both findings sat in `labeled_spelling_pins`, **two lines below a derived `len(LABELED_SET)`** — and **all three figures were CORRECT**, which is why four readings walked past them. Repaired **structurally** (`§5` r71) with a new `Instance.surface` axis — asserted to be a real second axis, agreeing with `expect_blind` on only 5 of 7 — and the derived output reproduces `4 of 7` / `4 of 4` / `3 of 7` **exactly**: the repair changed no fact, only who computes it. Post-repair `literal = 0`. **The iter then published two unmeasured figures in its OWN progress.md** (`51 modules / 8 sections`; `41 → 52`), caught by running the commands before committing — recorded, not erased, because prose is exactly what this census does not reach. — see iter-199/progress.md
- iter-200 (tik): the milestone's oldest open harden route — *were the size-preserving mutation proofs vacuous?* — answered by census, and the answer has **two halves that point opposite ways**. **(1)** `§5` rule 77's hazard needs **re-imported Python source** (a `.md`/`.go`/`.yml` has no bytecode to go stale), and all **25** repeated in-place rewrites in this repo target data files — **0 target `.py`**. Structurally absent, not merely unobserved. The repeat predicate's blind spot is **bounded from above rather than disclaimed**: **35** test functions write a `.py` path, **none** bumps mtime, and four are the mutation batteries' `_stage` helpers — safe because each case gets a **fresh tmp directory**, i.e. **safe by isolation, not by any stated discipline**, one refactor from gone. **(2)** Rule 77 is **not the whole hazard**: **14 in-process `path → content` memos across 4 guards** (`_git_out` ×2, `service_repo_map`, `tracked_basenames`, `_SUBSTRATE`, `_BASENAME_INDEX`, `_HISTORY_CACHE`, `_REF_RESOLVES_CACHE`, `RESOLVE_ROUTES`, `NOT_CITATIONS`) defeat a mutation control regardless of mtime, size **or file type** — and exactly **one test module in the repo has ever called `cache_clear()`**, covering two of the fourteen. Bumping an mtime does nothing for the larger surface. The memo population is **enumerated, never asserted to zero** — one of those caches is worth ~10 s per guard run. Side: `route_disposition_guard` was **RED at HEAD** from iters 198/199 abbreviating a route id with an ellipsis; repaired before the iter opened. — see iter-200/progress.md
- iter-201 (tik): the milestone's **last open `FIX-` route** is closed, and with it the **25-test runner gap that has been inside every whole-population figure quoted since iter-170**. `test_claim_census_guard.py` — the repo's only module written as bare pytest-fixture functions, and the single member iter-197's census enumerated — now collects **29 tests identically under both runners**, with **no case body edited**: the cases are renamed `_case_*` and bound onto a `TestCase` **by generation from the module namespace**, which makes iter-182's measured *"the obvious translation loses tests silently"* failure **unrepresentable** — and asserted anyway from **two independent derivations** (namespace-vs-class both ways, plus a re-count of `^def _case_` in the file source). The three fixture APIs (`monkeypatch.setattr` ×6, `capsys.readouterr` ×6, `pytest.skip` ×2, measured before planning) are shimmed in ~40 lines and **the module imports pytest nowhere** — the root of the asymmetry, not a symptom. Census after: **124 modules · 3,580 · 3,580 · gap 0 · 0 DISAGREE · testcase 124**, and iter-197's registry reported its own entry **STALE without being asked**. Emptying that registry makes every live declared-vs-derived arm compare two empty sets, so the fence was **repaired rather than left green** (`§9`): the firing proof moved wholly to the synthetic battery, the empty registry declares itself a closure, the style arm gained its positive half, and the offsetting-members arm was **kept** — a zero total is evidence only when its parts are zero. One fence failed on its own body (`assertNotIn("import pytest", src)` in a method containing those words). — see iter-201/progress.md
- iter-202 (tik): **the nineteen exposed pairs are adjudicated — 18 false-RED, 0 false-GREEN, 1 not staleness at all — and the parser under them was truncating four extensions.** `materialize()` gained a `ref`, so a citation can be read at `origin/main` (`git show`, no network) instead of at a six-clones-stale working tree; `--exposure-adjudicate` puts both excerpts and the citing corpus unit side by side. The counter and the renderer disagreed **16 against 19** on their first joint run — a whole-file citation materializes to an empty body at both refs, so a body-comparing renderer read them as identical — repaired into ONE derivation both call, with the CLI exiting 2 if they ever diverge. Then every pair was decided: the corpus is correct at `origin/main` **18 times out of 18** and the stale clone is what would contradict it, which **refutes iter-198's guess** that false-GREEN was the likelier half (the corpus was written against origin in the first place; several of those blocks disclose the flip in their own prose). The 19th was not staleness: `db-backup.md`'s bare `storage.tf:24-38` resolved into the **`storage` service repo** because after the resolver discarded the directory there was exactly one file with that basename — **uniqueness is not correctness**, and no ambiguity guard fires at one hit. Repaired both sides, with the guard cut as narrow as the defect (guarding all rooted targets cost **434** pairs; guarding every repo-qualified miss re-labelled 21 honest ones; the shipped rule fires only where the old code would have RESOLVED — **1 pair, the whole class**). Chasing its apparent second member found the class underneath: `SOURCE_EXTS` is joined in human order and Python alternation is **leftmost-first**, so `.json`->`.js`, `.jsx`->`.js`, `.tsx`->`.ts`, `.graphqls`->`.graphql` at **every** occurrence — **63 targets mis-spelled and 35 citations never seen as citations at all** (tier-1 **3,090 -> 3,125**, materializing **582 -> 623**, unresolved **220 -> 167**). Censused across the instrument family: **4 alternations, 1 affected**, and the discriminator is **not ordering** but whether anything after the alternation can fail — the three safe ones have a `\b`, a required `:N`, an anchoring `$`; the unsafe one's follower is optional. Also `_git`'s `.strip()` was reading file CONTENT, which shifts a leading blank line and manufactures a `differs` out of an identical file (split into `_git_text`, mutation-controlled). **Side discovery:** running the complete importer set turned two arms RED on a **pre-existing** iter-201 residue — a converted module still declared `PYTEST_ONLY` and still carried as an OPEN blocker; removed, ratchet tightened to 0, and the emptiness now DERIVED rather than trusted. — see iter-202/progress.md
- iter-203 (tik): **the measurement-literal census now reads the site-kind `print()` hides — and its first find was a number iter-202 had invalidated one iter earlier.** iter-199's census walked `print(...)` calls only, recorded against itself as `SURVEY-M257x-iter199-the-literal-census-reads-PRINTS-only`; a number in a DOCSTRING rots identically and is read more often, because the docstring is where a guard states what it measured. `docstring_measurement_literals` enumerates that site-kind across rext non-test modules — **95 measurement-shaped numbers across 62 sites**, split **53 dated · 7 document-relative · 35 standing** — and **asserts on none of them**: `dated` and `doc-relative` are mechanically decidable, *"this present-tense sentence asserts a current property"* is NOT, and failing on it would grade prose (the over-reach `D-M257x-202-4` priced twice inside one iter). What is fenced is the **SIZE** — a ratchet the population may fall below and may not rise above without a reason — plus non-emptiness of both decidable classes and a **mutation control** that strips the dated marker from one sentence and requires the classification to move. **The census's own denominator was wrong in the first draft** (85 across 55, from a scratch probe that did not apply the ordinal rule) — *quoting a throwaway measurement beside a fence is precisely what the fence enumerates*, committed while describing it; corrected at both sites with provenance stated. **Its first member:** `basename_index`'s docstring carried *"205 of 695 line-pinned citations"* as a standing property; the tree derives **292 of 704** at distinct-citation-text grain and **410 of 979** at pair grain — both operands moved, the numerator mostly **because of iter-202** (un-truncating `.json`/`.jsx`/`.tsx`/`.graphqls` recovered 35 invisible citations and re-spelled 63, overwhelmingly bare filenames). **The missing unit is half the defect**: `205 of 695` is checkable against neither grain because it names neither. Repaired to state both and fenced by a test that recomputes both operands and asserts the docstring carries them, with an anti-vacuity arm requiring the two grains to actually DIFFER. The registry's completeness fence went RED on its own author for the **third** time in three iters within a minute of the new function existing — classified at source, and recorded as the registry working rather than as a chore. — see iter-203/progress.md
- iter-204 (tik): **the vocabulary both literal censuses see through was a hand-written list inside the module written to end hand-written lists — measured, it saw 22.2 %.** `_MEASURED_NOUNS` conditions everything either census reports, and since iter-203 it has had **two** consumers. It cannot be derived (the superset is *any* number followed by a word, and only a human can say `4 says` is not a count), so it gets the treatment `§5` prescribes for a declared population: **reach, measured against the superset, with the misses named.** Of **478** number+word occurrences in string literals across rext non-test modules the vocabulary matched **106 — 22.2 %**; after taking **37** measurement nouns from the corpus's own uncovered list (`blockers` ×13, `columns` ×6, `names` ×5, `citations`, `records`, `predicates`, `anchors`, `routes`, `dispositions`, `playthroughs`, `subtests`, `schemas`, `bytes`, …) it matches **183 of 483 — 37.9 %**, and the **addressable residual is ZERO**. The rest are verbs — `says`, `closes`, `misses`, `ships` — **written down** in `_NOT_NOUNS` rather than filtered, because a correct exclusion is still a defect while it is silent. Fenced three ways: residual == 0, a scan-size arm (a zero residual over an empty scan is not a measurement), and a **mutation control** that guts the vocabulary to one noun and requires the residual to reopen. **Then the widening separated two claims that had been indistinguishable.** iter-199's printed census read zero under a 29-noun vocabulary; 37 nouns later it **still reads zero** — so that zero was never an artifact of a narrow lens, which is the one thing a declared vocabulary always leaves open, and it is now pinned. iter-203's docstring census went the other way: **94 → 162, a 72 % undercount one iter old.** Same lens, same tree, opposite verdicts. The ratchet was re-baselined 95 → **162 — taken from the census, not from the dry run that predicted 160**, the two extra members being the reach audit's OWN docstring joining the population it measures; taking the simulation's number would have repeated `D-M257x-203-2` one iter after recording it. — see iter-204/progress.md
- iter-205 (tik): **the third site-kind — `#` comments — had no census at all, and closing it found the vocabulary was CASE-SENSITIVE.** A comment is a *token*, discarded before an AST exists, so neither existing census could ever see one — and the comment is where this repo writes its *"Measured: N of M"* provenance, the sentences most likely to be quoted as evidence. `comment_measurement_literals` reads them with `tokenize`: **118 measurement-shaped numbers, 95 of them `standing`** — a far higher standing share than either sibling (docstrings 73 of 164; prints 0 of 10), which is exactly what comments are for. The classification rule is now **one function with two callers**, fenced as such, because writing it twice was the available shortcut and is precisely the shape iter-202 paid **16-against-19** for; the three site-kinds **partition**, proven by staging one sentence three ways and requiring each census to see only its own. **Then iter-204's residual arm returned exactly one entry — `playthroughs::1` — for a word that IS in the vocabulary.** The occurrence is *"23 Playthroughs stayed green"*, and `_MEASURED_RE` was case-**sensitive** while the noun list is written lower-case: **every capitalised measurement noun was invisible to all three censuses simultaneously**, in a repo that capitalises Playthroughs, Stories and Heroes. **Nobody would have found this by re-reading the word list — the word was in it**; it took an arm comparing the vocabulary against what the tree actually writes. The reach audit itself turned out to be **string-literals-only**, i.e. carrying the identical blind spot it exists to find in its consumers — extended to comments, superset **483 → 799**, reach **38.4 %**, residual **zero**. Both ceilings (comments **118**, docstrings **164**) were taken from the census **after** the last change: the first comment scan read 101 across 89 sites, and quoting it would have been the third repeat in three iters of *a census's denominator must come from the census*. The registry's completeness fence went RED on its own author for the **fifth** time in four iters — recorded as the strongest available evidence that it has not fallen behind the tree. — see iter-205/progress.md
- iter-206 (tik): **the standing class gets its first VERIFIED subset — and four of the first six were stale, two of them made stale by this very run.** iter-205 sized the comment site-kind at **96 standing figures** and closed at the cap with one confirmed-stale instance unrepaired. Six of them sit in `claim_census_guard.py` and describe populations that module computes — the smallest subset where *derivation* is available rather than judgement, in the file this whole run has been editing. Derived: *"in these 40 files"* → **41**; *"a wholesale warning over 949 pairs"* → **1,015**; and **twice** *"a class of 2"* / *"Measured over the corpus: 2 pairs"* → **1**. **The two `2`s are iter-202's own number.** That iter measured the wrong-repo class at 2, wrote it into two comments, and then — four paragraphs further down the *same iter* — fixed the extension truncation that made the second member exist at all: `ant-academy.md`'s `code/public/catalog.js` was a **parser artifact**, the file being `catalog.json`. The class was **1** before iter-202 closed and its own comments still said 2 four iters later. **A number can go stale inside the iter that writes it**, and its author is the least likely person to re-read that sentence. The `949` was **not re-pinned to 1,015** — that buys one iter and re-arms the trap — it now points at `_exp["under_clones"]`, the value the code prints two lines below, and says in place that it read 949 from iter-198 until iter-202 moved it with **nothing noticing for four iters**: *a number a comment does not carry cannot go stale*. The other three are corrected in place and **fenced against the live census**, with an anti-vacuity arm requiring the three derived values (**41 · 1 · 1,015**) to be pairwise distinct so a comment carrying the wrong one cannot satisfy the wrong arm. The class now reads **168 sized · 7 derived · 161 unverified** — its first numerator — and the **4-of-6 hit rate is explicitly not an estimate for the remaining 161**: the subset was selected for being derivable, and derivable figures cluster in the modules under active edit, which is where staleness concentrates. — see iter-206/progress.md
- **harden pass 50 — CORRECTION to iters 205/206, recorded not erased.** The `standing` class this
  milestone published is **inflated by 21**. `comment_measurement_literals` handed the *shared*
  `_classify_measurement` a single tokenised **line**, while its docstring sibling hands it the whole
  string — so the ±120-char window that decides `dated` could never be filled from a comment, and a
  provenance marker one line above was invisible on exactly one site-kind. Re-derived at iter-206's own
  tree (`ede026d`) with iter-206's own vocabulary and the window fixed: comments **74 standing of 117**
  (published **95 of 118**), docstrings **73 of 164** (correct, unaffected). So the class reads
  **147 sized · 7 derived · 140 unverified**, not *"168 · 7 · 161"* — and iter-205's headline that
  comments carry *"a far higher standing share than either sibling"* is **partly an artefact of the
  window**: the share is real (63 % vs 45 %) and smaller than it looked. Note also iter-205 and iter-206
  published **95** and **96** for the same reading in adjacent lines. The rate is no longer quoted
  anywhere; `derivation_registry.classifier_window_miss_rate()` derives it, and the parity between the
  two site-kinds is fenced. — see hardening-ledger.md pass 48–50
- iter-207 (tik): **the three literal censuses drop every test module, and the dropped population is the bigger one.** All three share one line — `path.name.startswith("test_")` — stated twice as a *rationale* and never once as a *size*. Sized: **462 rows / 314 `standing` excluded** against **322 / 164 censused**, across **79 modules in five sections**. The rationale is right about ASSERTIONS (`assertEqual(len(rows), 7)` goes RED when wrong) and wrong about PROSE (a test module's docstrings and message strings are asserted by nothing); `§5` — *a membership check cannot see a hole inside a member*, and *a correct exclusion is still a defect while it is silent*. Shipped `excluded_test_module_literals` + a shared `_measurement_units` extraction **proved to reproduce both siblings with an empty symmetric difference** (319 = 319, 0 either way) rather than an agreeing total. The pre-registered six (sealed in this iter's first commit, `91ee86f`) came back **3 of 6**, and the split is structural: rows naming a value some function still computes were all live; rows naming a past measurement were all mis-graded as claims about today, because `_classify_measurement`'s ±120-char dating window is narrower than a test module's header-to-figure distance. `dating_window_sensitivity()` now derives that exposure on every run — **93 of 314 test-side (29.6 %), 119 of 164 non-test-side (72.6 %)** — so the `standing` bucket is an **upper bound**, not a count, in BOTH populations. One row was stale in both senses at once: *"25 modules declared a `FENCE_KIND`; 23 were enumerated"* against a live **27 / 27**. Repaired by **self-dating** (`§5` iter-111 — provenance inside the payload), never by re-pinning. `_MEASURED_NOUNS` **+1** (`class(?:es)?`), forced by the residual arm — *a vocabulary derived from a population with a hole in it inherits the hole* — which widened both siblings by construction (**179 → 181**, **137 → 141**). +6 arms, 2 mutation controls fired with sha-verified restores, 4 further REDs on this iter's own author. **91 passed** (changed fence) · **314 passed / 1 skipped** across nine modules, pytest 3.9.6, `stack-core` only, Python only. — see [`iter-207/progress.md`](iter-207/progress.md)

- iter-208 (tik): **the milestone's own NOT-COVERED clause names ten Python sections; the repo derives four.** `TOK-08` turned on the scope disclaimer itself. Counted over `hardening-ledger.md`: *"the ten non-`stack-core` **sections**"* appears **once** and is correct (11 on disk, minus `stack-core`); *"the ten non-`stack-core` **Python** sections"* appears **five** times and is wrong — six of the ten carry **zero** `test_*.py`, which is what `LANGUAGE_EXCLUDED_SECTIONS` says about each. **One adjective was inserted into a correct sentence and the number was never re-derived**, then copied verbatim through five passes and into this run's own orchestration prompt. **A second, independent error:** *"NOT COVERED"* was true of the pass that wrote it and was read as *"never read"* — while the same ledger holds iter-145's table and two harden passes' tables of those very sections being run. `§5` iter-178 one step later: **a clause that WAS a measurement, copied forward until it read as a property.** Re-read them, tree frozen, pytest 8.4.2 / CPython 3.9.6: `demo-stack` **9 failed · 1,063 passed · 2 skipped**, `dev-stack` **151**, `stack-injection` **335**, `stack-verify` **275** — **1,824 passed and all 9 failures are exactly the 9 declared `ENV_GATED` entries, so 0 undeclared.** Root cause of the rot: `suite_census` derives `go_sections()` from `go.mod` and `ts_sections()` from `playwright.config.ts` and had **no Python derivation at all** — `derive_sections()` is a subtraction over the exclusion registry, the shape `PYTHON_TEST_GLOB`'s own note forbids — so the set the clause names had nothing to be graded against. Shipped **`python_sections()`** (the collector's own glob) + 3 arms including an **independence** control that stages a language-excluded section carrying a Python test: mutating the glob back to the registry subtraction takes **that arm RED and leaves the agreement arm green**, which is the finding restated as a control. `§5` gains the rule *a NOT-COVERED clause DECAYS — it names a RUN and gets read as a PROPERTY*. **42 passed / 1 skipped** on the changed fence. ⚠️ The five wrong clauses live in `hardening-ledger.md`, which this skill is **forbidden to write** — routed to the next harden pass by name. — see [`iter-208/progress.md`](iter-208/progress.md)
- iter-209 (tik): **the intra-corpus citation census reads 94 of the repo's 2,560 markdown documents, and five of the ones it skips are bound to the corpus by `CLAUDE.md`'s own maintenance contract.** `TOK-08`, turned on its own first-and-largest class. `corpus_citation_guard` reports `C2 anchor resolution 193 … OK` — sound **inside its source set**, which is `corpus/**` + `README.md` + `CLAUDE.md` and had never been stated as a denominator. Same shape as iter-208 one level up. `CLAUDE.md` § *Interconnected Documentation* opens *"These files must be maintained together:"* and names **eleven** documents, **five of them under `.claude/skills/`** — the fence reads the file that names them and not the files. `§5` iter-184: **a fence's POPULATION is a registry too.** Measured BEFORE widening, with the guard's **own** machinery so the source set was the only variable: **20 skill docs, 135 citations, C1 133 of 133 resolve, ONE C2 finding** — `stack-secrets/SKILL.md` links `safety.md#…-v16-**m27m28**` against a heading that reads **M27–M30**; the range grew, the heading followed, the anchor did not. **Zero false REDs**, which was the stated precondition for widening at all. Also repaired **four broken anchors this run had just written** (iters 207/208 linked `#tok-08-…-stop-sampling-them` against a heading carrying a trailing `— 2026-08-07`) and a **unit error in iter-208's own headline** (*"derives five"* where the clause says *non-`stack-core`* = **four**; five includes `stack-core` — `§5` r75, left visible in place). ⚠️ **Instrument note worth more than the finding:** this iter's first independent reading used a hand-written slugger and returned **97 unresolved of 190** — **16× wrong, entirely in one direction**, because it collapsed double spaces and stripped `_` while the shipped `heading_slugs` deliberately emits two variants. iter-201's 18-false-RED/0-false-GREEN shape again, and the reason the audit reuses the guard's machinery: when the question is about **scope**, hold the **machinery** fixed. Also sized and rejected: **23 location-keyed registries, 165 keys, 0 dangling** (the 19 non-resolving are basenames and platform-relative paths, both by design) — a clean zero, routed. Shipped `SKILL_SOURCE_GLOB` + 3 arms whose contract list is **parsed out of `CLAUDE.md`, never restated**, with a staged mutation control proving the arm can fire. Guard after: **1,801 citations over 114 sources, 0 findings** (was 1,666 over 94). **22 passed** on the changed fence; removing the widening takes **both live arms RED** and leaves the staged control green. — see [`iter-209/progress.md`](iter-209/progress.md)
- iter-210 (tik): **iter-209 widened one of TWO identical source-set derivations, and the family had no way to notice.** `grep -l "def collect_sources"` returns two modules holding the **same four lines**, the second under a comment reading *"**Kept identical on purpose**: two fences over the corpus that disagree about what the corpus IS would each be measuring a different denominator."* The intent was right and the mechanism was a duplicate: **four minutes after iter-209 the family answered 114 and 94 to one question**, symmetric difference exactly the 20 skill docs. `§5` iter-190 named both the defect and the repair — **two readers of one construct must SHARE the derivation; agreement today is not the property** — and a comment asserting two copies are kept identical is the strongest available evidence that nothing was checking. Shipped **`fence_provenance.corpus_sources()`**, one derivation in the module the whole family already imports; both fences route through it. Measured on the replaced copy: sources **94 → 114**, pins **2,193 → 2,201**, findings **3 → 3, zero new** (a *findings* change was pre-registered as the stop condition and did not fire). +3 arms fencing **the sharing, not the agreement** — and the sharing arm found a **third** private corpus walk within a minute of existing: `clone_drift_guard` at two sites. **Not folded in**, and the reason is measured: the shared set adds **22 documents carrying 73 sha-shaped tokens, 1,454 → 1,527 occurrences (+5.0 %)**, `CLAUDE.md` the densest — a behaviour change, not a de-duplication. *remember the scope* has now failed four times, so `test_03` **varies the pool and asserts the sensitivity** instead of restating the lesson. ⚠️⚠️ **And a shipped fence went RED on this session's own writing:** `route_disposition_guard` found **`SURVEY-M257x-h42` CLOSED at iter-200** and **published open by iter-219** nineteen iters later — because the premise came from `hardening-ledger.md`, which re-listed it as *routed forward* in passes **48, 49, 50 and 53**, every one after the closure. **The ledger is a SECOND disposition surface the route registry does not read**, so the two disagreed for six passes and an iter spent its opening claim on it. Corrected in iter-219's block in the registry's own grammar, appended not substituted — **57 closures · 0 contradictions** after; iter-219's actual product stands as an **independent reproduction** of iter-200's answer by a different instrument. **179 passed / 0 failed** across five modules (pytest 8.4.2 / CPython 3.9.6, `stack-core` only, Python only, 2 m 34 s), `route_disposition_guard` exit 0, ceiling re-pinned **563 → 566**, `--ceilings` exit 0. — see [`iter-221/progress.md`](iter-221/progress.md)
- iter-222 (tik): **the tooling ships its own declaration of the platform's topology, and nothing had ever read it.** `rosetta-extensions/demo-stack/clones.pin.json` — the canonical clone pin `ensure-clones.sh` seeds into every fresh workspace and `DEMO_ADVANCE_CLONES=pinned` checks out — named **11** repos. `repos.yml` @ platform `origin/main` (`0c91421`) names **4**; with the two sanctioned extras (`platform`, which cannot list itself, and `ant-academy`, absent by design per M49 #5) that is **6**. **Five keys were phantom** — cms · jobsimulation · storage · messenger · roadrunner — every one a repo whose removal `repos.yml`'s own header states in the platform's words. A `grep` for `clones.pin` across every `.py` in the repo returned, apart from doc-comments, only `test_tooling.py`, which writes *synthetic* pins: the real file's contents were asserted by **nothing**, in either repo, for four releases. `clone_pin_guard.py` (layer 7) now fences it three ways — no phantom, **no hole** (`pinned` leaves an unpinned repo UNTOUCHED, so a missing key is a repo the barrier silently does not cover), and no moving-branch ref — reusing `platform_alignment_guard.parse_repos_yml` rather than re-deriving the clone set. 16 tests, 0 skipped, a per-phantom mutation battery and four anti-vacuity controls; `guard_family` proved its own registry first by going **exit 2** naming the unregistered guard. **Freshness is deliberately NOT asserted**: the pin is M246's reproducible *barrier*, 3-of-6 behind origin when measured (app **28** · next-web-app **12** · ant-academy **9**), and advancing it is a bring-up decision with a re-proof attached — what was repaired is the comment *asserting* it was "current-origin/main", a dated claim that names a run and is read as a property. **And the finding the iter did not go looking for: nobody had fetched.** `anchor_construct_guard` + `repair_postcondition` resolve corpus anchors at the app clone's `origin/main`, which pointed at the clone's **own HEAD** — both green. One plain `git fetch` moved it 28 commits and the same two guards went **RED with 9 anchors** across four corpus docs, no corpus file changed and no guard edited. *A remote-tracking ref is a cache, not a remote.* Written into `§8` as a standing rule with its three consequences. **The iter tried to route the 9 forward and the tooling refused** — `repair_postcondition`'s pre-commit hook blocked the close outright (*"a repair may remove these; it may never add one"*), so the repair landed here: a fence that only advises is a fence you can plan around. All nine adjudicated **unpinned** first (a ref-pinned site read at origin/main would be the *guard's* error, `§5` rules 41/44), then re-derived by locating the same construct at `3eaadae68` — never by adding an offset, and the offsets are not uniform (`app/main.go` +0/+1/+14/+24, `backend.go` a flat +5). **And the floor caught 9 of a 15-member cluster:** `anchor_construct_guard` detects *"resolves to nothing"*, never *"resolves to the WRONG construct"*, so six siblings had rotted onto real-but-wrong lines invisibly — repairing only the flagged nine would have left a half-repaired citation block that reads as verified. All six previously-failing modules re-run together: **256 passed, 0 failed**. — see iter-222/progress.md
- iter-223 (tik): **`demopatch` is the only way a demo changes platform source — 23 sha-pinned manifests standing between the platform's code and a working stack — and nobody could ask whether the SET still applies.** `demopatch preflight` has answered that for **one** manifest against the **checked-out** tree since M217; the set-and-ref question had no cheap form, so the only way to learn the answer was a bring-up and a warning line ~11 minutes in — which is exactly how *"a silently-refused perf patch shipped a 76 s members grid for four releases"* (`§5-bis`). `patch_anchor_guard.py` censuses every manifest at a **named ref** and asserts the two conditions `demopatch`'s own G2 refuses on: the path exists, and the anchor occurs **exactly once**. **The answer to the question that motivated it — would advancing the clones break the patch layer? — is NO: 23/23 anchors resolve at `origin/main`**, against clones iter-222 measured at app **+28** / next-web-app **+12** / ant-academy **+9** behind it. **And the second reading is the finding the probe could not pre-register:** run at `HEAD` the guard returns the same **10** sha drifts, and the two sets are **identical member for member** — those baselines were already stale at the ref the demo builds *today*, so the platform advancing did not cause them and advancing further will not worsen them. At least one is stale **by design** (`§6`'s urls.ts chain, where one patch's `pre_sha256` IS another's `post_sha256`); classifying the other nine is routed, and **the guard reports the count while stating that it does not report the cause**. The design decision worth defending: **sha drift is counted, printed inside the verdict, and never a finding** — M217's gate self-heals on an intact anchor, so a fence reddening on drift would contradict the shipped mechanism and go RED on a set that works; pinned by a test asserting the *non*-behaviour. 15 tests fire every reachable verdict (anchor gone · ambiguous · path gone · a broken sibling beside a green member) plus four anti-vacuity controls. **Three fences then caught this iter's own work:** `platform_predicate_guard` went RED on iter-222's *"the pin named 11 repos"* (its G2 arm reads a number beside `repos` as a clone-set claim) — repaired by **rephrasing to "11 keys", not waiving**; three literal ratchets breached and their arrows had to be **split** between writing and **reach** (the iter widened `_MEASURED_NOUNS` by `manifests`/`paths`/`shas`/`drifts` — the sixth time the vocabulary has closed on the sentence that widened it), isolated by holding the TREE fixed and varying the MATCHER, with `COMMENT` the clean control at exactly **zero** prose contribution; and the derivation registry rejected an entry in **both** directions inside one hour — an ungraded site, then a graded site that does not exist. — see iter-223/progress.md
- iter-224 (tik): **four of the six clones nobody had fetched had advanced — and what landed in them was the platform correcting this corpus's own claims.** `ROUTE-M257x-222-other-clones-never-fetched` closed. Sealed pre-fetch, then refuted: `P-224-1` predicted **≤2 of 6** advanced; measured **4** — `storage` **+20**, `messenger` +7, `jobsimulation` +4, `cms` +2. The census of the corpus's citations into them found **0 broken of 92** (and corrected its own sealed denominator: `\b<repo>/` also matches `app/internal/cms/…`, so 150/123 → **92/78**, 58 false). But line-ranges are the weak arm: **three load-bearing M810 claims resolve ONLY at origin** — `messenger/terraform/main.tf:29` reads `container_definitions` at the graded clone and `service_desired_count = 0` at origin; `jobsimulation/terraform/main.tf:15-22` is an atlas block vs the M810 comment; `storage/terraform/main.tf` is **100 lines vs 18**. **The corpus was right and its substrate was wrong** — it cites both the old HEADs and the new tips (`4ce8ece` 7 hits *and* `9f8cb53` 9), so it already knew refs its own clone set did not. The mirror image of iter-222: staleness there made a **rotted** claim read green, here it would have made a **correct** claim read red. `P-224-4` held and sharpened: `clone_drift_guard` returns `OK`/exit 0 against **both** substrates, because D1 asks only *"is HEAD a sha the corpus cites"* — a clone parked on an old cited sha satisfies it exactly as well as one at the tip, so **not-fetching is a way to pass**. Repaired: the four clones advanced to their tips, and the retracted **"rollback path"** claim corrected at both its sites — `messenger/terraform/main.tf:27-28` still says *"a one-line revert… not a re-provision"* while the **same commit** (`459b184`, `e9421c6`) rewrites `messenger/CLAUDE.md` to record the `production-messenger` ECR **DESTROYED 2026-08-05** with four siblings (infrastructure #3253; six repositories left in eu-west-1), so restoring is *"a re-provision, not a revert."* **A resolved anchor quoting a verbatim line is not the source's position.** — see iter-224/progress.md
- iter-225 (tik): **gate clause 1 is not gradeable on the sanctioned dev host, and the blocker is not the bring-up.** The working-stack half of the redirect, sized by asking the cheapest disqualifying question first. `D-v28-15` (2026-07-31) supersedes `D-v28-14` **the same day**: `billion` demo-only, **`odysseus` RETIRED**, dev/test LOCAL to the new Mac. But `hostprofiles/` holds exactly two profiles and **neither describes that host** — `laptop.json` is a 10-core / 16 GiB M1 Pro (9,937 MiB VM, 58 GiB disk), `billion.json` an 8-core x86_64 containerd box; this host is `Mac16,11` arm64, **12 cores / 24 GiB**, overlay2, VM **8 CPU / 11.67 GiB / 125.4 GiB**. **Both profiles name `odysseus` as the gate host** citing the superseded `D-v28-14`, and **no `odysseus.json` was ever written** — so the named gate host has no profile and the real one has none either (`P-225-1` HELD; `P-225-3` HELD — the doc names billion **44** / odysseus **26** / laptop **22** / the sanctioned host **0**). **`P-225-2` REFUTED, and the truth is worse in one direction:** `require_measured` is clause zero for a `None` *measurement input* (a dead sampler), **not** for a missing profile. `--profile` is operator-supplied with **no host auto-detection**; a *missing named* profile is correctly exit-2, but a **present and inapplicable** one is graded **silently** — `host_facts()` is written into the run JSON at `:905` and **never compared to the profile**, while the same harness already refuses an `autoverify.json` verdict *"that does not describe the run under test"* (`:809`). **The discipline exists and is pointed one object too late.** `P-225-4` HELD: the VM floor is **12 GiB binary** and this VM is **11.67 GiB**, so the bring-up warns every time (−2.75 %; the decimal-slider unit trap iter-12 already documented). **Disk is NOT the constraint** — 64.97 GiB free against a 25 GiB requirement; **CPU saturation probably is**, and `laptop.json`'s own history is the precedent (a full cycle **attempted and REFUSED**, `peak load1 10.69 > cores-2`, because *"a developer workstation is not a bench"* — which is now the sanctioned dev host). Repaired: a superseding banner over `build-budget.md`'s stale `D-v28-14` block carrying all six measured consequences (0 corpus-scoped citers, so nothing re-pointed). **The unblocker is a measured `Mac16,11` profile from a quiet box, not more bring-up work.** — see iter-225/progress.md
- iter-226 (tik): **a decision recorded 35 times in the plan had reached the corpus zero times.** Census of the `D-v28-14` → `D-v28-15` supersession iter-225 surfaced. At `5393ba2`, `D-v28-15` occurred **0×** in `corpus/` + `CLAUDE.md` and **35× in `knowledge/`** — `state.md:15`, `roadmap.md:301`, and **this milestone's own `exit_gate`**, which names the retirement in the gate text (`P-226-1`, `P-226-4` HELD). **The decision was recorded thoroughly and never crossed the boundary.** `P-226-3` HELD: `CLAUDE.md:435` — the file every session loads — asserted in the present tense *"the gate host is now `odysseus`"*, about a host retired 9 days earlier. `P-226-2` HELD: of 20 `odysseus` lines outside iter-225's banner, **~15 are LIVE** claims (campaigns *"run on"* it `:170`, its baseline *"UNMEASURED — M257 owes it"* `:177`, *"the gate"* `:417`, `odysseus.json` owed at `:352`/`:369`/`:574`); exactly **1** (`:459`, a probe record) is correctly historical and left alone. Repaired the two a blanket banner **cannot** cover because they are ACTIONABLE: `CLAUDE.md:435` (line-count neutral), and `build-budget.md:573-575` — a **runnable, copy-pasteable** `buildbench run … --profile odysseus` introduced by *"until it lands this line cannot run"*, for a profile that **will never land** — now `--profile <your measured host>` plus which profiles exist and the iter-225 fact that **nothing compares the profile you name to the machine you are on.** ~13 residual prose sites counted and routed, **blocked behind** the same missing-profile blocker as gate clause 1. **And this iter's own seal was published wrong**: `grep -c` counts LINES, so *"26 in build-budget.md"* was a line count against 26-before/32-now occurrences — total survives by coincidence, basis did not; corrected in place. **A supersession that reaches the PLAN and not the CORPUS has not landed either** — the third face of the milestone's retraction rule. — see iter-226/progress.md
- iter-227 (tik): **asked all six archived repos what they say about themselves — two carry a freeze banner and four still describe live, ECS-deployed services.** Generalising iter-224's lesson (*"read the repo's retraction surface"*) into a check. `messenger` and `storage` ship **⚠️ FROZEN** banners and **agree** with the map; `cms` calls itself *"Content layer for the platform… via GraphQL Federation"* with **no status statement**; `jobsimulation` likewise, though `6092c6d2` destroyed its ECS service and ECR; `graphql-wundergraph` says *"it is **currently the sole subgraph**"* with no mention that `2adcf71` deleted it, **and** carries the `2 → 1` figure this corpus corrected to `3 → 1`; `roadrunner` advertises *"Deploy: Docker -> ECR -> ECS"* and returns **zero** hits for any status vocabulary. **`P-227-1` REFUTED (2 of 6, predicted ≥4). `P-227-2` REFUTED — and that IS the deliverable**: zero repos state a status the map lacks, so **the messenger defect is BOUNDED to one site**, which iter-224 had no way to know. `P-227-3`/`P-227-4` HELD — every gap is the repo being behind, never the corpus wrong on direction. **So iter-224's method has a measured two-in-six hit rate**, and the trap is aimed at this corpus's own reader: an agent cloning `cms` or `roadrunner` to check a claim is told the service is live. Landed as a boxed 6-row section in `platform-migration-status.md`. **The fence caught this iter twice, both correctly** — `derived_count_guard` arm D on *"4 of 6"* (an `N of M` on the clause-5 surface with no `N_OF_M_DISPOSITIONS` entry; cleared by spelling it *"four of the six"* rather than pushing an rext registry widening for one sentence) and `repair_postcondition` with it. **And the insertion moved 27 lines out from under three citations while the family read 24 GREEN** — `anchor_offset_guard` needs `--range`, so an un-ranged family green is NOT an offset check; found by grep, all three re-pointed against the rows they name (`dependency_map.md` `:88→:115` app, `service_taxonomy.md` `:89→:116` cms, `services/README.md` `:101→:128` intelligence). — see iter-227/progress.md
- iter-228 (tik): **the corpus's most-cited fact was stale, and the corpus already contained its own correction.** `CLAUDE.md` asserted *"origin/main is now `ad9f3c49`"* for `app` — the repo it cites **174** times — in the same paragraph that warns *"Cite the sha, never the moving label."* Measured: `app`'s origin tip is **`3eaadae6`** and `ad9f3c49` is **28 commits behind** it (`P-228-1` REFUTED). **And `corpus/ops/observability.md` already said so** (*"`origin/main` was `3eaadae6` on 2026-08-07"*), as did `shared_libraries.md`'s go.mod measurement — so **`CLAUDE.md` was behind the CORPUS, not the platform, and nothing compares the two.** Full 13-clone census against **origin tips**: `next-web-app` **12** behind + tip cited **0×**, `ant-academy` **9** behind + **0×**, `rosetta-extensions` out of scope (tag-pinned consumption clone); the other nine at 0 behind (`P-228-2` HELD, 3 of 13). **`P-228-3` HELD and completes iter-224's reading of the guard:** `clone_drift_guard` returns **OK/exit 0** with the flagship repo 28 commits behind, because D1 grades **local HEAD** and `ad9f3c49` is cited 174 times — iter-224 showed staleness *satisfies* it, this shows the same green beside a 28-commit gap. **The three ACTIVE clones were deliberately NOT advanced** (unlike iter-224's four archived ones): advancing `app`/`next-web-app` changes what a demo BUILDS, which is exactly what `ROUTE-M257x-222-pin-advance-needs-a-reproof` gates behind clause 1's cold cycles — that route now carries measured numbers instead of an open question. Repaired both `CLAUDE.md` sites, line-neutral, **keeping the original claim pinned to the ref it was measured at** (swapping the sha would have silently re-attributed *"all five anchors resolve identically"* to a ref where it was never measured). **This iter's own first census pass used the wrong operand** — local HEAD instead of origin tip — and would have published *"all 13 tips cited"*, a clean bill of health from a column that cannot answer the question; published rather than silently fixed. **And `anchor_construct_guard` RED'd the repair itself**: naming a second document inside a block carrying a bare `:504` continuation pin re-bound that pin — the exact trap `observability.md:13-22` documents, walked into while citing it. — see iter-228/progress.md
- iter-229 (tik): **`buildbench` graded every run against an operator-named host profile and never asked whether that profile described the host.** `host_facts()` was collected into the rep ledger at `:905` and read by no assert, while `read_verdict()` one screen away already refuses an `autoverify.json` that *"describes an earlier stack"* — the discipline was there, pointed one object too late. Landed `profile_describes_host()`: three verdicts (`match`/`mismatch`/`unmeasured`), three arms (`kind`, `arch`, `cores`), wired into `run_campaign` before the reps loop and before `--dry-run` short-circuits, with a hatch that records the gap in every rep ledger and **never buys a zero exit**. Both checked-in profiles are now correctly REFUSED on the sanctioned dev host. **6 of 6 sealed predictions HELD**, and `P-229-6` came back stronger than written: `os.cpu_count()` is **12** here, `docker info` NCPU is **8**, and `billion.json` — an x86_64 *native-Linux* profile — declares `cores: 8`, so the cheap "compare cores to the engine's NCPU" version **silently accepts billion on an arm64 Mac**; the cheap "compare arch" version silently accepts `laptop`. Hence three arms, each pinned by a regression test that opens by asserting its coincidence still exists. Three whole-section failures, all this iter's own, all repaired: (a) the refusal returned **2**, which `main` already spends on *"could not run at all"* — reserved `EXIT_HOST_IDENTITY = 3` and taught M256's host-robustness test its third outcome; (b) **+199 lines rotted 22 corpus anchors and the instruments caught 3** — 1 by the literal census, 2 by a blank line, **19 silent**, exactly the floor `anchor_construct_guard` prints on every run and which nobody had costed; (c) the re-derivation **carried its own numbers across a later edit** of the same file. Offsets were non-uniform (+1 / +195 / +199), so a global bump would have written 22 new wrong numbers. `§8` gains *"before comparing a DECLARED number to an OBSERVED one, ask what the declared number is a number OF."* — see `iter-229/progress.md`
- iter-230 (tik): **the corpus's commit shas had never been censused.** 138 distinct shas across **1,497 citation sites** in `corpus/**` + `CLAUDE.md`, resolved with `git cat-file -e` against 14 platform clones + this repo + the rext authoring copy: **132 of 132 measurable resolve, 0 demonstrably wrong**, and the instrument was proved in BOTH directions before that zero was published (3 fabricated shas resolve nowhere; 3 real `HEAD~3` shas resolve to exactly their own repo). **3 of 4 sealed predictions REFUTED** — 138 not ≥150; `platform` (34) outranks `app` (21) as the most-cited repo; and short-sha ambiguity is a **non-hazard at 0 of 138**. The first pass reported 14 unresolvable and **eight were the instrument's own missing clones**, caught before publication. The **6 residuals are UNMEASURED, not false** — every one names a repo in **no clone set on this box**: `infrastructure` (`13c248e6`, **61 sites**), `db-backup` (`7dd1b80` 11, `6e1fb15b` 4), `ant-singularity` (3), `colony` (2), `ant-observability` (1). **82 citation sites rest on evidence this box cannot re-read**, headed by the very commit that settles the standing **cms M810** question and which `CLAUDE.md`, `corpus/README.md`, `service_taxonomy.md`, `platform-migration-status.md` and `org-repos.md` all lean on — iter-123 cloned `infrastructure` to establish it and the clone is gone. `§9` gains the partition rule: **an unresolvable reference is two findings wearing one symptom — a wrong citation, or a repo you do not have — so partition on clone-set membership before reporting a rate.** No clone was fetched to close it (`D-M257x-230-2`: the clone set is deliberately frozen behind gate clause 1) — see `iter-230/progress.md`
- iter-231 (tik): **the class iter-228 found by hand is now enumerated, and it is nearly empty.** A BROAD pattern (any sha within 90 chars of `origin/main`/`HEAD`) returns **75 sites / 28 shas** and looks damning — `ad9f3c49` at 21 sites against `app` `origin/main` `3eaadae6`. **Nearly all of it is the corpus writing its refs CORRECTLY**, in the dated past tense this milestone spent iters teaching it (*"which was `origin/main` on 2026-08-06"*); grading those stale would punish the fix. The STRICT population — present-tense assertions that a sha **is** a repo's current ref — is **6 sites: 4 AGREE** (roadrunner `87d8d44`, studio-room `aeec036`, and `CLAUDE.md` twice on `app` `3eaadae6`), **1 UNMEASURED** (`ant-observability`, uncloned — iter-230's partition rule), **1 STALE**: `platform-alignment.md:1476` calls `2adcf71` *"Origin HEAD"*, and platform `origin/main` is `0c91421d`, **6 commits ahead**. **Repaired by DATING the phrase, not by bumping the sha** — the paragraph's whole argument is that iter-21's corrections were false *at that ref*, so the sha is the claim's SUBJECT; bumping it would destroy the claim to fix its tense (`D-M257x-231-2`, the mirror of *repair the citation against the subject, never bump the offset*). The extractor was proved BOTH ways before the count was published: it fires on all four present-tense shapes and **correctly skips** the three dated past-tense forms it is required to skip — without that control, 6 is indistinguishable from a broken regex. **2 of 4 predictions REFUTED**, including `P-231-4` (*the corpus is split on some repo*) — refuted because **iter-228 had already repaired the only instance**, a prediction written from a three-iters-old finding against a corpus that had moved. **`ROUTE-M257x-228-corpus-disagrees-with-itself-about-refs` CLOSED by measurement**: 0 repos carry two different claimed-current shas — see `iter-231/progress.md`
- iter-232 (tik): **on the FILE axis the corpus is aligned — 0 of 111 cited platform paths are gone.** 111 distinct `(repo, path)` pairs across **345 citation sites** in `corpus/**` + `CLAUDE.md`, resolved with `git cat-file -e origin/main:<path>` over 13 repos (`app` 73 of them). The first pass reported **11 missing and ALL 11 were the instrument's**, failing in four distinguishable ways: a literal **ellipsis** the corpus wrote (`app/.../resolver_queries.go`); **`app/` as a DIRECTORY inside `studio-desk`** (3 paths); **studio-room paths written as `app/studio/…`** because CI embeds that repo in the app **image**, so the corpus cites an image path (4); and **shorthand** — `jobsimulation/ai/ai.go` abbreviating a full `internal/jobsimulation/ai/ai.go` stated one line above. - iter-241 (tik): **the same green meant two different things — the clone set is an INSTRUMENT and nobody had stated its reach.** `TOK-08` on `ROUTE-M257x-236-host-is-the-unreliable-witness`, open since iter-236 and never worked. `platform_alignment_guard` derives its unclonable set from **disk presence** (`not (clones_root / head).is_dir()`), so *"the repos no stack clones"* is a fact about **the machine that ran it**. Run against the **same corpus** on two clone sets: this laptop (**13** clones) → **11 of 109** citations NOT checked, 2 repos excused; a fresh bring-up's set (**7** clones — `repos.yml`'s four + the two `clone_pin_guard` sanctions + the rext consumption clone, symlinked into a temp root and passed to the guard's own CLI) → **27 of 109**, 7 repos excused. **Both exit 0.** The guard checked **16 fewer citations** and the only thing that said so was a number nobody was comparing, in a sentence carrying **no denominator and no roster**. **Why they differ, and why the laptop is the anomaly:** `stack-demo` holds 13 clones, 7 accounted for, and the other **6** — `cms`, `graphql-wundergraph`, `jobsimulation`, `messenger`, `roadrunner`, `storage` — are exactly the repos `repos.yml`'s own header calls frozen legacy that *"`make init` therefore does not clone… clone them by hand"*; they are **pre-`838d907` leftovers**, and `ensure-clones.sh` adds only `ant-academy` + `platform`. **So the workspace used longest verifies the most, and the cold machine the exit gate describes verifies the least — seniority reads as coverage.** **The exposure is far larger than the fence's subject:** the guard grades ONE file (the map, 109 citations) while the corpus carries **107 citations into those same six repos** outside it (`cms` 41 · `jobsimulation` 21 · `messenger` 15 · `roadrunner` 12 · `graphql-wundergraph` 10 · `storage` 8), including the merge-banner anchors this milestone leans on hardest — **graded by nothing**. Landed: the verdict now states **numerator, denominator and the clone-set roster** plus *"a different clone set gives a different reach under the same green"* — and it had to ride **inside** that sentence, because `guard_family.run_one` reports exactly that line for a green member. **Two regression tests** (file 64 → **66**): one pins the shape, the other builds the restricted set by symlink and requires the unchecked count to **RISE** while the denominator — a property of the map, not the box — **stays at 109** (`D-M257x-241-3`), so a regression making `M` clone-set-dependent fails a check a naive "the number got bigger" test would pass. Corpus disclosure in `platform-alignment.md` §8. **The 6 leftovers are NOT deleted** (`D-M257x-241-2`) and the clone set is **NOT grown** (`D-M257x-241-1`) — curating a clone set to make a fence green is `§8`'s fetch rule one level out, measuring a memory of the platform rather than the platform. Seals **3 confirmed / 1 refuted / 1 partly confirmed**, and **the refutation is the most useful line in the iter**: `P-241-2` predicted the excused count would rise 2 → **8** and it rose to **7**, because **`graphql-wundergraph` is cited 10× corpus-wide and not once in the map** — I compared a count derived from the corpus's population against a guard whose population is one file. **Fourth denominator error of this lineage, and the first where both numbers were mine.** Guard family **26 GREEN / 0 RED**. — see iter-241/progress.md
- iter-242 (tik): **the family audited against a fresh bring-up's clone set — 2 of 26 members are clone-set-sensitive and BOTH are honest — and the run's most dangerous instrument bug arrived with no symptom at all.** `TOK-08`, closing out `ROUTE-M257x-236-host-is-the-unreliable-witness`, which iter-241 narrowed but left open over *"the other 30 family members"*. Ran the whole family twice against the same corpus and diffed member by member: this box (13 clones) **26 GREEN · 0 RED · 0 could-not-check · 5 not-run**; the restricted set (7 clones) **25 GREEN · 0 RED · 1 could-not-check · 5 not-run · 1 off a fallback rung**. Landed a **non-fatal** `NOTE` naming both clone sets and stating that a symlinked alternative does not take effect — non-fatal because symlinked workspaces are legitimate and **the defect was the silence, not the resolution** (`D-M257x-242-1`) — plus 2 tests (`test_guard_family.py` 52 → **54**). - iter-243 (tik): **`--reload-sentinel` restarted a `demo-N` container on every `dev-N` stack — and the tested gate is exactly what hid it.** `TOK-08` closing `ROUTE-M257x-239-stackseed-sentinel-reload-is-demo-only`, opened at iter-239 while grading consequence and routed rather than worked. `shouldReloadSentinel` fires on **any** non-prod stack with `n > 0` — its own doc says the `n > 0` clause exists to skip *the dev stack at N=0*, which puts **`dev-N` for N > 0 explicitly in scope** — while `reloadStackSentinel` took **only `n`** and reconstructed `fmt.Sprintf("demo-%d", n)`. The RPC leg was never wrong (offset-keyed, `8087+n`); the **`docker restart` fallback** named `demo-N-sentinel-1`, a container a dev-only box does not have, and since the seed has already succeeded and the step is best-effort the operator gets a warning about the **wrong container** while the re-seeded `g2`/`g3` grants **never reach the running enforcer** — the silent-403 class `verification.md` exists to catch. **Invisible because `blueprint.ParseStackN` returns only the OFFSET** (everything after the first `-`, prefix discarded), so the family was gone before the function was called — it could not have known, only guessed, and it guessed `demo`. **Third finding this run tracing to that same root**: iter-239 found the corpus *teaching* the unqualified spelling at 5 sites; this is the code that cannot recover from it. Repaired by **keeping the name rather than re-deriving it** (`D-M257x-243-1`); an unqualified `--stack 1` or empty target still falls back to `demo-N`, so **the demo path is byte-identical** — this iter's stated escalation condition (`D-M257x-243-2`). **The gate was already tested and the container NAME was not, which is exactly how it survived**: `TestShouldReloadSentinel_GatePredicate` has **8** cases including `high-n-demo-reloads` — every one asserts the boolean, none asserts what the reload then does. Two tests added, the second the more important: one covers both families + both fallbacks, the other pins the **premise** that the gate is family-blind, so a future demo-only gate fails loudly instead of leaving the new function silently unreachable. Seals **3 confirmed / 1 refuted**, and the refutation is a **stop** signal: the hard-coded `demo-` shape occurs at **exactly one site** in the whole section, so there is no class to census and no fence for a population of one (`D-M257x-243-3`) — `§5` iter-168 used in the direction that says do less. Whole `stack-seeding` Go suite green (**12 packages, 0 failed**). — see iter-243/progress.md
- iter-244 (tik): **the SEVENTH runnable input, and the first that grades the SUBJECT rather than an argument.** `TOK-08`. - iter-245 (tik): **the platform slice measured ZERO — and the zero paid for something better.** `TOK-08`, working iter-244's route. Sealed first (`2a51389`) with 5 pre-registrations + the substrate: **732 path-qualified `path:NN` citations across the 114 live docs — 335 repo-rooted with the clone PRESENT (218 single-line + 117 range), 131 repo-rooted with the clone ABSENT (`infrastructure` ×45), 203 repo-relative, 63 rext-or-corpus.** Graded at each clone's worktree AND `origin/main`: **4 distinct paths looked missing and ALL FOUR were the instrument** — head collisions, a first segment naming a real repo while meaning something else (`app/` is a directory inside **studio-desk**; `jobsimulation/ai/ai.go` is a mid-sentence abbreviation of `app/internal/jobsimulation/ai/ai.go`, spelled in full two clauses earlier). One of them defeated a citing-document disambiguator because **the sentence named the repo and the document did not**. So the class measures **ZERO** on this slice — `anchor_construct_guard`'s 599-strong out-of-reach bucket is genuinely out of reach, not a hiding place — **and the same 4-of-4 reading is why the platform slice must NOT get iter-244's fence** (`D-M257x-245-1`): a rext section is an unambiguous prefix, a repo name is not, so an existence fence here is a false-RED generator. **A measured zero is a licence not to build.** What the zero paid for: **117 of the 335 were RANGE citations and 117/117 resolved**, while the guard was reporting **490 ranges, 24.8 % of its qualified population, in NEITHER of its counts** since harden pass 59. `ROUTE-h59` was routed whole because *which line of a range carries the claim* has a 490-anchor blast radius — **but whether the file HAS that many lines never did.** Shipped the **range-bounds arm**: 490 ranges go from ungraded to **`349 resolved and BOUNDS-checked, 141 refused`** (refusals head-keyed like the single-line bucket), gating at zero, with the disclosure stating **both** halves in one line so a green on the graded half cannot read as coverage of the ungraded one. **3 real defects on its first run, one class:** a bare basename inside `corpus/services/<svc>.md` that the sentence says means a DIFFERENT repo — `README.md:81-87` meaning **platform's** (storage's is 33 lines) and `main.go:518-523` meaning **app's** (storage's is 18); - iter-246 (tik): **the milestone's longest-standing structural lead, censused to completion — and its diagnosis corrected.** `TOK-08`. `ROUTE-M257x-238` was **six-for-six**: six consecutive iters each found a `CLAUDE.md` code fence contradicting `CLAUDE.md` prose, blamed on `/update-knowledge` sweeping prose and not fences. Sealed first (`32c4c02`) with the population: **11 fenced blocks / 55 runnable command lines / 12 comment lines — and nobody had ever read all 55.** The census: **CLEAN, 0 defects in 67 lines.** All 10 platform make targets declared; `app/atlas.hcl:6` really declares `env "local"`; sentinel declares `initdb proto` and **no `gen`**, so the *"NOT `make gen`"* warning is right; studio-desk's `vite.config.ts:10` and `.env.example:4` carry exactly the cited 9100/9000; ant-academy's `dev` is `next dev --port 3077`; and `dev:web` → `scripts/dev-server.sh` → **`PORT=8555`**, a three-hop chain that checks out. **`P-246-1`'s falsification branch fires arithmetically and its stated conclusion is REJECTED** (`D-M257x-246-1`): it assumed the alternatives were *structure* or *coincidence*, and there is a third — **the six repairs held, and nothing was left behind to catch the seventh.** The generalisation is the finding: **iters 235–238 censused four runnable-input surfaces, repaired every defect, and fenced NONE of them.** *A class is not closed by a repair; it is closed by an enumeration that keeps running* — `§8` iter-176, which this milestone wrote and did not apply to its own censuses. Two further holes were caught by the guard's **own unit tests before it shipped**, both in its *excusing* logic rather than its asserting logic: a fabricated `stack-nowhere/` was silently substituted to a real workspace (**a pardon travelling with the repo name**), and arm A could never fire for any `stack-*` path. The refusal line is principled — **a repo-level miss is REFUSED** (`chronos` decommissioned, `experiments` hand-cloned — indistinguishable from a typo, iter-244's rule cutting the other way) **and a sub-path miss FIRES**, because once the repo is located its interior is knowable. **3 of 5 pre-registrations refuted or rejected**, including P-246-2 (every comment-borne claim checked out) and P-246-4 (all 7 `cd` targets exist). `ROUTE-M257x-238` **CLOSED**; new `ROUTE-M257x-246-two-of-four-censused-surfaces-still-have-no-fence` — env vars and frontend scripts/ports remain unfenced. — see iter-246/progress.md
- iter-247 (tik): **the env-var surface fenced by INVERTING it.** `TOK-08`, taking iter-246's route and `ROUTE-M257x-237`, which called this *"the strongest fence candidate"* and proposed the **wrong instrument**. The obvious rule — *every env name the corpus mentions must exist in `.env_example`* — is refuted by the substrate in one measurement: of **327 distinct `UPPER_SNAKE` tokens / 1,213 occurrences**, the most-cited are variables the platform **DELETED** (`CMS_RPC_ADDR` ×38, `SKILLER_RPC_ADDR` ×19, `STORAGE_RPC_ADDR` ×18). **A must-exist fence would redden the corpus's most careful writing, because absence is the point of those sentences.** So the polarity is inverted (`D-M257x-247-1`): fence the **absence** claims, which have exactly one reading and which the platform can falsify at any time. **Three measurements, each of which killed a draft.** (1) Single-variable absence claims **mis-attach**: 12 variables carry an absence phrase over 29 sites and **6 of the 12 are actually PRESENT** — `AUTHORIZATION_ADDRESS` at `docker-compose.yml:48`, `JUDGE0_BASE_URL` at `:59`, `MESSENGER_ENABLED` at `:87`, both `STORAGE_S3_*` at `:82`/`:83`, `GRAPHQL_SCHEMA_FOR_GEN` at `.env_example:105` — and **all six are co-location artifacts**, the phrase belonging to a neighbouring clause (usually *"zero `*_RPC_ADDR` variables anywhere in compose"*); `sentinel.md:85` says `AUTHORIZATION_ADDRESS` **is** set, in the same sentence. **0 of 12 absence claims are false.** (2) Restricting to lines with exactly ONE env token leaves **2 sites and still mis-attaches**, because the real subject is the **glob**, which an `UPPER_SNAKE` pattern cannot see. (3) **The glob was the subject all along and is the stronger claim** — family tokens in an absence sentence: 13 distinct over 41 sites, dominated by **`*_RPC_ADDR`, asserted absent at 23 sites across 15 documents.** That is the corpus's most-repeated platform claim, made as service after service folded into `app`; **one re-added RPC address falsifies 23 sites at once**, and nothing was watching. Shipped **`env_absence_guard`** (FENCE-M257x-iter247) — **17 family-absence claims hold, zero findings** — with three measured scope rules: the claim must **name its own file** (23 of 41 do; the other 18 are frontend/rext, where platform-compose grading is a false RED), a ***read by nothing*** claim is about **CODE** and is refused as a category error, and **a bare `no` is not an absence quantifier** — it produced the guard's only two findings, both false (*"no rebuild needed"*, *"with no `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` … override"*), one of them in a sentence asserting the **opposite**. Plus: a commented-out setting is not a setting, and a bare mention is not a setting. **17 tests**; and the guard's own test **improved the guard** — it now names the offending variable, not just its location. Family **28 → 29 GREEN**. **P-247-1 REFUTED** (6, predicted 2–4). `ROUTE-M257x-237` **CLOSED** — though not as written: the presence list stays hand-maintained and dated, and this iter's measurement says a presence fence over it is the wrong instrument. **The corpus was right and undefended.** — see iter-247/progress.md

The tree moved under the instrument** (`D-M257x-248-4`); *an instrument that states its own invalidity must not exit 0* applies to a measurement I take, not only one I audit. - iter-250 (tik): **two corpus fences were grading the operator's box and calling it the corpus.** `TOK-08`, taking iter-249's frozen clone pair as an instrument. Sealed 5 pre-registrations first (`099a947`). **`rext_path_guard` returned OPPOSITE VERDICTS on the live tree and on a clean clone of the same sha** — `rc=0` here, `rc=1` there, printing *"1 reference(s) name a path that does not exist in the rext tree: `.claude/skills/stack-list/SKILL.md:33: demo-stack/stacks/registry.json`"*. The citation is **correct**: `demo-stack/.gitignore:8` is `stacks/`, so the provenance registry `/stack-list` documents is **runtime state**, present on a box that has run a demo and absent on a fresh clone. The guard graded it by **existence**, which is a property of the box, and accused the corpus. Repaired with `git check-ignore --stdin` — which decides *pathnames*, existing or not, so the answer comes from the **tree** — into a **RUNTIME** bucket printed by name on every run, plus a `RUNTIME BUCKET UNDECIDABLE` disclosure when git cannot answer (`D-M257x-248-3`). Live and frozen now return the **identical verdict**. Second: **`fence_command_guard`'s GREEN carried no reach** — derived through the guard's own `locate` (never a second parser, iter-209's 16×-wrong lesson), of **103 graded `cd` occurrences 0 are reachable from a bare checkout of this repo, 1 is in the tooling clone and 102 (99.0 %) need a provisioned `stack-*/` workspace**; distinct-arg unit **25 of 26 (96.2 %)** — *name the unit*. The split is now printed on every run and asserted to sum to its denominator. 8 new tests; **the anti-vacuity control failed on its own fixture first** and caught that the section set is derived from rext's directories. **3 of 5 pre-registrations held** — PR-3 refuted into a tier the taxonomy lacked, and PR-5 refuted structurally: the workspace tier is invisible on a box with no workspace, so a reader-facing reach measure needs a text-shaped classifier (routed). Family **29 GREEN / 0 RED / 5 not-run**, unchanged. — see iter-250/progress.md
- iter-251 (tik): **the same defect one guard over, at 21× the size — and there turn out to be TWO fresh checkouts.** `TOK-08`, on `ROUTE-M257x-250-the-runtime-bucket-is-one-guard-wide`. Sealed 5 pre-registrations first (`dbedcea`). `corpus_citation_guard` — the family's largest citation fence, **1,808 citations over 114 documents, C1 1,606 / C2 196 / C3 6** — grades three clauses with `tp.exists()` against a tree whose `.gitignore` excludes `.agentspace/`. Censused through the guard's own resolver (never a second parser): **133 distinct paths tested for existence, 21 of them git-ignored**, all `.agentspace/rosetta-extensions/…`, all present here. The control that decides it: a `git clone --local --shared` of **`rosetta` alone** at `971cdc4` → **`RED — 21 of 1808`, rc=1, and all 21 are false**; the corpus is right and the reader simply has not cloned the second repo. **On iter-249's frozen PAIR the same guard is clean** — so *"a fresh checkout"* is two different trees that give opposite answers, and every reading in this milestone must now name which (`ROUTE-M257x-251-two-trees-both-called-a-fresh-checkout`). Repaired by **delegation**, not exemption: `git check-ignore` partitions out-of-subject targets, printed by name on every run, with `DELEGATION UNDECIDABLE` when git cannot answer — and the word carries an obligation that was **proved before the repair shipped**, each delegated path looked up in `rext_path_guard`'s own reference set: **21 delegated · 21 owned · 0 orphans**, now the live test's answer key. Live and fresh-checkout verdicts are now **identical**. 5 new tests incl. a control keeping a tracked missing target RED. **5 of 5 pre-registrations held** — the run's trend is 1/5 → 3/5 → 5/5, the difference being whether the claim was about a mechanism already measured. - iter-253 (tik): **the fresh-checkout class went from a count to a named census — and the control caught five REAL REDs hiding inside it.** `TOK-08`, on `ROUTE-M257x-249-fresh-checkout-hostile-tests`, the largest thing iter-249 left open. Sealed 5 pre-registrations first (`1f1e0be`). The tempting instrument was built and measured **first, so it could be refuted before it was designed in**: a static text predicate (*a test file naming `stack-dev`/`stack-demo`/`.agentspace` with no skip idiom*) selects **8 files, 6 in `stack-core`**, against a dynamic class of **13** — and **7 of those 13 never spell the paths at all**, so no text scan can see them. `test_toolchain_floor_guard` had already settled it from the other side: it **carried** a `skipUnless` and failed anyway, having declared **half** a precondition.
