- iter-254 (tik): **the run-to-zero began, and the direction I was NOT aiming at is where every finding came from.** `TOK-08`, on iter-253's named class. Sealed 5 pre-registrations first (`b2de62d`). Phase A **read** the real failure of all 22 on the frozen pair (**45 s** — a loop cheap enough to run per edit, which is what made the iter possible) and refuted `PR-1` immediately: the class needs **two** preconditions, not one — **the clone set** (the corpus cites files that live in git-ignored clones; `UPGRADE-IMPACT-next16.md` lives at `stack-demo/next-web-app/`, so its absence prints *"resolves in neither pool and is undeclared"* about a corpus that is right) and **an installed `node_modules`** (three arms read *"the TypeScript population fell to 0 tests"*). **The second was invisible to inspection** — `test_prune_census_can_return_NON_ZERO`'s own assertion message already contains it: *"the tooling repo, **which has a node_modules**"*, a precondition written down as an assumption. Repaired **8 arms across 6 files**, of which **five already declared HALF a precondition** — keyed on a threshold a fresh checkout does not quite hit *because something is always gradeable*: `rc == 2` when iter-250 measured **1 of 103** commands reachable from a bare checkout; `total == 0` when `total` is **1**; `not LIVE` when `LIVE` asks only whether the **corpus** is present, and it is. **A skip rung that has never fired is not a declaration.** Then **`PR-5` was refuted by this iter's own code**: `node_modules_present` globbed two levels — enough from the rext root, not from the **rosetta** root that one call site passes — so it read `False` on a machine that has one and a **passing test began to SKIP**. *A test that stops running looks exactly like a test that passed*; the **live** half of the verification caught it, the diff did not. Fixed, bounded (this root carries ~180,000 files), pinned by a both-roots regression test. Also proved a **cascade**: the mutation battery asserts its own baseline is green, so `test_18`/`test_22` failing failed it three times over — **2 of the 3 passed with no edit of their own**, and the third did **not**, so it is routed rather than decorated (`D-M257x-254-4`: do not decorate a cascade, it retires a working control). **Class 22 → 12** across 7 files, verified in both directions (frozen: 8 skip with the precondition named; live: 8 run and pass, 135 passed / 1 pre-existing skip across the touched files). The predicates ship in `suite_census` **beside the census that names the class** — the rule that flags a test and the rule that excuses it must be one rule. **3 of 5 pre-registrations held** (trend **1/5 → 3/5 → 5/5 → 4/4 → 2/5 → 3/5**), and for the first time this run both refutations are about **my own work** — `PR-3` on scope, `PR-5` on a defect. Side-deliverable: all three literal ratchets re-pinned, **the COMMENT arrow breaching the ceiling it was written to raise**. — see iter-254/progress.md
- iter-255 (tik): **`ROUTE-M257x-249` CLOSED at zero — and the mutation battery caught what the closing did to it.** `TOK-08`, finishing the run-to-zero. Sealed 5 pre-registrations first (`666c3fb`), three of them predicting against a clean sweep. The residual **12 each declared their own precondition** (no cascades this time), and the class is now **0 failed / 20 skipped** on a frozen clone pair and **22 passed / 0 skipped (1,070 s)** live — verified in both directions, as every repair in this route has been. **Only two preconditions exist across the whole 22 — clone set 19, `node_modules` 3** — with no third cause and no non-environmental member, read one failure at a time over the full population, so it is a **bounded negative** rather than an exhausted search (`PR-1` refuted; `PR-5` held, so iter-253's control stands over every member). The residual's symptom was a **collapsed denominator** rather than a named missing target, which is why it looked like a different class and was not: *"resolution collapsed — the zero is vacuous"* (**136** against a floor of 200), *"the widened denominator collapsed"* (**30** against 150), *"resolves in neither pool and is undeclared"* (**15** citations, every one a real file inside a clone). And the **half-precondition** shape held to the end — every one of these arms already skipped on `if not (root / "corpus").is_dir()`, a check a fresh clone **satisfies**, because the corpus is exactly what a fresh clone has. **The last member paid for the iter on its own:** `mutation_battery::test_01`'s failure text **changed mid-repair**, from *"the declared-GREEN control went RED"* to **`THEATRE: mutant 'anchor-header-lookahead-dropped' left the suite GREEN`** — and that verdict is **correct**, because once the detecting arms gained preconditions a fresh checkout skips them and the mutant really is undetected there. The battery was not broken by the declarations; it **measured** them. So `D-M257x-255-1`: *a mutation battery is evidence only where the arms that would detect the mutation actually run*, and every precondition added anywhere narrows the trees on which that battery's GREEN means anything — `§5` rule 77 arriving from a new direction, routed as `ROUTE-M257x-255-a-declaration-narrows-every-mutation-proof` across the ~30 proofs in `hardening-ledger.md`. Separately worth its own severity (`D-M257x-255-3`): **two of these arms do not merely mislead, they INSTRUCT** — one names two **real** exemptions as fiction to retire, the other says *"report it as a corpus defect"* about 15 citations that are all real. **2 of 5 pre-registrations held** (trend **… → 3/5 → 2/5**), and **all three refutations are pessimistic misses** — booked on the same terms as optimistic ones, with the cause legible: iter-254 paid for the predicates, so the marginal member fell to three lines. New: `ROUTE-M257x-255-the-class-can-regrow` — the class is at zero and **nothing watches it**; pairs with `ROUTE-M257x-253-the-iter-loop-runs-no-ratchet`, and the two want one cheap close-step between them. — see iter-255/progress.md
- iter-256 (tik): **the advance is TAKEN — and the measurement that was supposed to precede it refuted its own premise.** `TOK-08`, on the user's 2026-08-10 closing condition, recorded binding as `D-M257x-256-1` before any work: the milestone closes only when CURRENT `main` assembles into a working stack, **demo AND dev**, with deprecated repos no longer treated as live — which makes advancing the clones **in scope and required**. The re-survey moved a number in its first minute: the brief's `ant-academy` **+9** was a cached remote-tracking ref; a real fetch says **+10** (`D-M257x-256-2`). Built `advance_impact_census.py` — for every corpus citation resolving into a clone behind its own fetched `origin/main`, the cited **line's text** is read at BOTH refs and classified into seven buckets, taken **through `anchor_construct_guard`'s own resolver** (five wrapped functions, positional pairing **asserted** rather than assumed) so the population is never a second parser's. **Two partitions published side by side** — a block naming a sha is a ref-scoped claim, and so is one naming more than one: **CONSERVATIVE 33 impacted of 172 in subject** (23 corpus sites / 9 documents / 29 distinct platform lines; `app` **23.0 %**, `ant-academy` 7.7 %, `next-web-app` **0.0 %**), **PERMISSIVE 118 of 372** (53 / 16 / 69; 36.4 % · 19.4 % · 0.0 %). `held` 139/254, `dead` **0**, `born` **0**. An ordering defect in the census was found and fixed inside the iter — with the pin tests ahead of the not-advancing test, one exclusion bucket read **78** under one partition and **183** under the other on the same tree, which would have made the two partitions incomparable. **Then the repair was built, applied to 27 citations across 18 sites, and REVERTED — and that is the iter.** The applier is fail-closed (one pass per line, because `storage.md:29` carries eight `main.go` anchors and the map contains a rewrite of 504 to 517 *and* of 517 to 530; an occurrence-count refusal; a hyphen guard keeping RANGE citations invisible; a hard refusal of `moved-ambiguous`/`changed`). Reading the six refusals killed the premise on the third: **`platform-migration-status.md:121` is entirely `origin/main`-framed** — its *"`MESSENGER_ENABLED` … read at `:1459` and `:1576`"* names the two `if messengerEnabled {` lines at `origin/main`, which sit at **1445** and **1552** in the checkout — so seven **correct** citations had just been moved onto comments. **The corpus is a TWO-CLOCK document and the second clock is our own tooling** (`D-M257x-256-3`): `anchor_construct_guard`'s `CITE_REF=auto` tries `origin/main` first, so guard-derived anchors are `origin/main` numbers and clone-read anchors are checkout numbers, adjacent and unmarked — `observability.md:28` is `origin/main`-framed and **`:29`, the row directly below it, is not**. All 27 were reverted by inverse-applying the same instrument and `git diff` came back **empty**, which is also the strongest evidence available that the rewriter edits exactly what it claims. `--apply` now **REFUSES** without `--adjudicated <file>`. **The advance was then taken deliberately** — three clones fast-forwarded **and the canonical `demo-stack/clones.pin.json` advanced with them**, because the pin is what a cold bring-up reads and a checkout-only advance is undone by the next `ensure-clones.sh` (`D-M257x-256-4`). All six clones now `behind=0`; `clone_pin_guard` GREEN. **`clone_drift_guard` went RED on the advance — the fence firing on a real event for the first time in this milestone** — naming `ant-academy` and `next-web-app` as at a sha the corpus never cites (24 and 40 citing sites), while `app` did **not** fire because the corpus already cites `3eaadae6`, one more sign the corpus is ahead of the checkout rather than behind it. Cleared with two **measured** records: ant-academy's `PUBLIC_CHAPTERS` **499 → 554 (+55)** (open-source and finance families, EN+IT) **plus the inference that does not follow** — the demo's *"65 course cards"* counts a filtered subset of the **skill-path** objects, unchanged at **92**, so the advance moved chapters, not cards; and next-web-app `v2.137.0 → v2.137.3`, whose 12 commits are the AI-Readiness ⇄ assignments theme `app` moved **in the same window** (the coordinated multi-repo shape Trap D warns about). **Not claimed:** that anything builds — the `app` advance carries a new migration and a terraform fix reading *"the backend migration pipeline has been a silent no-op since the atlas 0.7.0 bump"*, and clause 1 needs a quiet host. **2 of 5 pre-registrations held** (trend **… → 3/5 → 2/5 → 2/5**); `PR-5` is the one to read twice — **0** guards flip at `CITE_REF=origin/main` because `auto` already tries it first, so the family has been grading at `origin/main` all along, is green there, and is **blind to all 118**. New `§5` **rule 79**. **Side discovery, and not this iter's defect** (`D-M257x-256-6`): the whole-section run — the first since iter-252 — came back **3 failed**, all three one inherited defect. `test_m257x_mechanical_fences_mutation_battery.py` gained `import suite_census` at iter-255 on a `sys.path` carrying only `tests/`, so **the harness holding ~30 of this milestone's mutation proofs has been UNREADABLE to pytest from the rext root for four iters** — invisible because the instrument that grades it only runs in a whole-section sweep no iter performed (`ROUTE-M257x-253`'s second witness, now dated and costed). Fixed at the import; the section closes **2,159 passed / 0 failed / 3 skipped (2,025 s)**, and that figure **re-pins the milestone's stale 2,096 baseline**. — see iter-256/progress.md
- iter-257 (tik): **the fence and the mechanism were reading different files, and only one was watched.** `TOK-08`, on `ROUTE-M257x-256-workspace-pin-is-not-the-canonical-pin`. Sealed 5 pre-registrations first (`95dd2e2`). `clone_pin_guard` has fenced `demo-stack/clones.pin.json` since iter-222; `DEMO_ADVANCE_CLONES=pinned` reads `stack-*/clones.pin.json`, a copy `ensure-clones.sh` seeds **copy-if-absent** (`:204`) and never reconciles — so a workspace created before the iter-222 phantom removal never receives it. Measured: this box's copy named **11** repos to the canonical's **6**, the five phantoms (`cms`, `jobsimulation`, `storage`, `messenger`, `roadrunner`) among them, **all five with directories present on disk carrying git checkouts**. Censused the readers by which file they mean: **two production sites read the copy** (`:234` the advance, `:377` the freshness report), both in one file, zero other files, and **zero fences**. Shipped **arm D**, and the asymmetry is the design (`D-M257x-257-1`): a **phantom key is a FINDING**, a **value difference is a DISCLOSURE** (`ensure-clones.sh:373` names the copy an *optional operator pin declaration*, and clobbering a deliberate declaration would be worse than the staleness), **no `stack-*/` is NOT-RUN with its reason printed**. Watched **RED → GREEN**: 5 findings + 3 disclosed drifts, then 0 after this box's copy was reconciled 11 → 6. **The three drifts were not an operator declaration** — they were the pre-iter-256 seed, so `pinned` on this box would have checked the clones **back out at the pre-advance shas while logging `pinned`**. `ensure-clones.sh` gained a divergence disclosure at the seed site (behaviour unchanged; the silence was the defect). **And the iter retracted its predecessor** (`D-M257x-257-2`): iter-256's *"the pin changes what a cold bring-up on any box builds"* is **false at all three publishing sites** — `DEMO_ADVANCE_CLONES` defaults to **`0`** and nothing outside `ensure-clones.sh` sets it, so a default bring-up applies **no pin at all**; a fresh box takes each repo's default-branch tip from `git clone` + `make init` and an existing workspace builds whatever its clones are checked out at. The **actions** were right; the stated reason would have justified advancing only the pin, which changes nothing a bring-up consumes. Then the iter's own edit **moved cited lines** (`D-M257x-257-3`): inserting the disclosure shifted `ensure-clones.sh` and turned **three guards RED from one cause** — `anchor_construct_guard` (`:467` landing on a closing `}`), `demo_knob_guard` (STALE ANCHOR at `:212`), `repair_postcondition` — re-pointed `demo-up-defaults.md` `:212 → :220` and `:467 → :475`. **§7 rule 4 applies to our own repo, not just to platform clones.** **4 of 5 pre-registrations held** (trend **… → 2/5 → 2/5 → 4/5**); `PR-4` missed — the pin turns out to be the **only** canonical rext artifact seeded copy-if-absent. Three ratchets re-pinned, and the third for a reason worth keeping: **a `#:` re-pin reason is itself a `#` comment, so writing down why a ceiling moved moves this ceiling.** **And the whole-section run paid for itself for the second iter running** (`D-M257x-257-5`): `test_test_collection_fence.py` caught that arm D's six tests had been appended **after** `test_clone_pin_guard.py`'s `__main__` guard, so direct execution would skip them **and still print `OK`** — *a test that stops running looks exactly like a test that passed*, arriving through a third mechanism after `skipUnless` preconditions and a memoised precondition. Section closes **1 failed / 2,164 passed / 3 skipped (2,937 s)**, the 1 being exactly that, fixed and re-verified both ways (**58 passed** across the file and the fence that caught it; direct execution `OK`). New route: `ROUTE-M257x-257-lock-file-is-unfenced` (`clones.lock.json` is graded by nothing and measured stale in 2 of 5 entries). — see iter-257/progress.md

- iter-259 (tik): **the DEV half is blocked by another project's only copy — measured before acting, not after.** `TOK-08`, on `ROUTE-M257x-258-no-dev-stack-on-this-box`. Sealed 5 pre-registrations first (`97bb83d`), then measured **read-only; nothing under `stack-dev/` was written, cloned, checked out or built.** The tooling puts a dev stack in **one** workspace — `dev-stack:53` `PLATFORM_DIR="${PLATFORM_DIR:-$REPO_ROOT/stack-dev/platform}"` and `migrate-dev.sh:19` `DEV="${DEV_CLONES:-$REPO_ROOT/stack-dev}"`, **two scripts behind two variables** (PR-2) — and there is **no per-N dev workspace at all**: `dev-N` is a port offset over one shared clone set (PR-3). **`stack-dev/` is another project's ACTIVE workspace:** `studio-desk` @ `795a411d`, **367 commits on no remote ref**, `origin` push disarmed to **`no-push://demo-clone-never-pushes`**, and — the fact that settled it — a **linked worktree** at `stack-dev/.worktrees/studio-desk-feat-stack-migration` @ `411a3c15 [release/3.2-full-frame]`, i.e. in-flight work with a second checkout into the same object store. And `platform/Makefile:22` clones only `if [ ! -d … ]`, so a documented `/dev-up` **adopts** that tree as a platform build source rather than cloning a clean one (PR-1) — iter-258's own demo log printed the behaviour verbatim (*"studio-desk already exists, skipping"*). **`make init`'s skip-if-present is an ADOPTION, not a no-op**, and it reads like a convenience. Three paths were each examined rather than dismissed: run `/dev-up` as documented (**rejected — unrecoverable downside on work that exists nowhere else**); relocate via `PLATFORM_DIR`+`DEV_CLONES` (mechanically possible, **rejected not on effort but because inventing a non-standard dev topology to satisfy a gate produces a result nobody could later trust**); move their work aside (**not this agent's call**). So `D-M257x-259-1`: **`user-blocker`, escalated BEFORE acting**, with a short decidable question — park the studio-desk work, authorise a relocated dev stack, or rule the dev half satisfied elsewhere. Also `FIX-M257x-259-stack-dev-N-is-not-implemented`: **`CLAUDE.md` advertises `stack-dev-2/` as "a secondary dev stack" and the tooling has no such concept** — a corpus claim no mechanism backs, this milestone's own class. **4 of 5 pre-registrations held, PR-5 SPLIT** (`D-M257x-259-2`): it bundled *"non-`main` branch"* with *"commits on no remote"*, and the branch clause is **refuted** — the clone is on `main` — while the substance held and then some. **Booked as a split rather than rounded to 5/5, because a prediction naming two things and scoring a hold when one is wrong is a prediction that cannot fail** — `D-M257x-258-3`'s class arriving one iter later, this time in my own grading. PR-4 held (first free N is **3**; `demo-2` now holds slot 2). The asymmetry between the halves is the finding: the demo half took one bring-up because nothing was in its way; the dev half has a live obstruction and the obstruction is someone's only copy. — see iter-259/progress.md
- iter-260 (tik): **gate clause 1 is MET — three consecutive `--purge` + `up` cycles, all green — and the count was corrected DOWNWARD before any of them ran.** `TOK-08`, on the two lines that do not depend on the user's dev-half decision. Sealed 5 pre-registrations first (`b7795b0`), the first of which **retracts the briefed count**: clause 1's unit is written as *"a cold `demo-down --purge` + `demo-up`"*, and iter-258 ran the **bare** `up-injected.sh 2` into a slot whose containers had never existed — a fresh-slot `up` with **no preceding purge**, as its own close records. So the strict count entering this iter was **0 of 3**, not the briefed 1 of 3, and sealing that before measuring meant the reading could not be shaped by the outcome. Three cycles then ran back-to-back, identical bare invocation, **no retries and no re-run for a nicer number**: **529 s / 458 s / 498 s CONTENDED**, each `{"warnings":0,"green":true}` with `EXIT_CODE=0` and **45/45 asserts ✓** (`public.skills = 42790`, `casbin_rules = 1251`, 11/11 containers each time). **4 of 5 pre-registrations held; PR-4 split** — its *"broad band around iter-258's 717 s"* is **REFUTED**, the three clustering tightly at 458–529 s (spread **71 s**) with all three **below** 717; the tempting *"warm cache"* attribution is **explicitly not made** (n=1 at 717, permanently contended host, nothing isolating cache from load) and only the negative is claimed — 717 s was not a usable expectation. **PR-5 is the one that mattered and it held**: three teardowns each freed `:27700` + `:23077` and each bring-up bound **exactly one** process on each (pids rotating `43878→67825→17970→95381`), so **M217's orphan class did not fire** — the half iter-258's fresh-slot bring-up could not exercise at all. `demo-1` is **byte-identical across all six transitions** (name + status + **container ID**), untouched as required. **Side-deliverable, committed separately at `dbe5c91`:** the orchestrator's mid-iter correction of the brief's `803` was **re-measured rather than relayed and went further** — `stack-dev/studio-desk` has **zero `origin/*` remote-tracking refs** (all 11 are `bundle/*`), so iter-259's `--not --remotes` subtracted a **local courier file** and its `367` is *"commits not in a bundle"* published as *"commits on no remote ref"*; `ls-remote` shows the alarm's own branch `release/3.2-full-frame` **`411a3c15` on origin, exact sha match**. A follow-on *"0 commits absent from origin"* was **computed and withdrawn before publishing** — the anti-vacuity control did not fire (the command was erroring against absent shas, `2>/dev/null | wc -l` rendering it `0`), so the other four branches are **UNMEASURABLE-FROM-HERE**, a third verdict rather than a clean bill. The `stack-dev/` prohibition **stands with its reason replaced**: staleness (local `main` `795a411d` vs origin `41ee3575`), not data loss. Protocol §8 widened — **"cannot push from here" is not "exists only here."** — see iter-260/progress.md
- iter-261 (tik): **clause 2 measured for the first time — and it is NOT MET: 29/31, one failing Playthrough, and the failure is a real product-side finding.** `TOK-08`, on `ROUTE-M257x-260-clause-2-never-run`. Sealed 5 pre-registrations first (`35e98c7`), including the **denominator**, which was in genuine doubt: the tooling is pinned **157 iters stale on purpose** (`D-M257x-258-1`, so the advance is the single changed variable) and the corpus's *"30 live"* is the count at M256 — measured at the pin, **30 `@pt` tags / 30 `outcome: success` / 1 `outcome: blocked`**, exactly what the clause names. `run-playthroughs.sh 2 --reset`, full suite, **no `--grep`, no `PW_WORKERS` override**, serial by config: the real reset path ran (**36-table FK-ordered TRUNCATE** then a fresh `pt-world` seed, not an additive re-seed), then **215 tests / 1 worker → 1 failed, 214 passed** in 162 s CONTENDED. Gate: **`29/31 passing (93.5%) — passing=29 failing=1 unimplemented=1`**, `GATE no-regressions FAILED`. Clause 2 asks **30 live / 0 failing / 0 error**; **29 ≠ 30 and one failing is not zero.** The failure is **`workforce-intelligence.talent-pool.UC1`** (`@pt:pt-workforce-succession`) — the manager's succession/at-risk view, `Expected: > 0, Received: 0` after a 15 s predicate timeout. **It is not environment or harness, and the page says so**: the captured text snapshot (no media opened) shows the surface **renders** — `heading "Succession Planning"`, correct org `Meridian Labs`, correct manager `Morgan`, its own `Good data coverage` and `At-risk people` cards — while **both projection tables render `img "No data"`**. It is **populated with chrome and empty of rows**, and its **three siblings pass on the same login, org, seed and run** (`workforce-roster`, `workforce-funnel`, `workforce-org-feedback` all `[PASS]`). So exactly one **computed projection** returns nothing. **Two candidate causes are named and neither is chosen**: a product change in how the projection is computed, or `pt-world` seed-contract drift at the frozen pin — discriminating them needs a pin bump (which would destroy the control) or an `app` source read, so it is **routed, not guessed** (`ROUTE-M257x-261-succession-projection-is-empty`). **4 of 5 pre-registrations resolved as predicted; PR-3 REFUTED** (pre-registered *at genuine risk*, and the risk was real). **PR-2 HELD and matters on its own**: the 157-iter-stale harness **ran to a verdict**, so the frozen pin is not too stale to measure with — the control is vindicated rather than merely tolerated. **PR-1 is graded UNTESTED**, not held: the run used `--reset` and the bare-`demo-up` counterfactual was never executed. `demo-1` `diff`-identical (name + status + container ID) **after a 36-table TRUNCATE on demo-2**. — see iter-261/progress.md
